## B. ROLE CONTRACT — DESTINATION vs ROUTE  (source: LORAMER_HANDOFF.md)
## ⛔ OPERATING DISCIPLINE — DESTINATION vs ROUTE (gate-level; read every session)
<!-- LORAMER_HANDOFF_OPERATING_DISCIPLINE_V1 -->
2026-06-23 FAILURE, named plainly: after correctly grounding in the repo, Claude converted ROUTE / sequencing decisions — its OWN job — into multiple-choice questions for Russ, and repeated it after being told to stop. Russ is NOT the coder or the plan-builder; offloading route decisions onto him is a FAILURE, not caution.

- DESTINATION vs ROUTE. Russ owns DESTINATION (what the app does/is; the governing law; genuine product forks the docs don't already answer). Claude owns ROUTE (how: sequencing, which platform/grain first, build order, whether to run a step the plan already specifies, blast-radius/safety). Claude DECIDES the route from the law + the plan, states a one-line reason, and proceeds — it NEVER asks Russ to choose the route.
- ASK RUSS ONLY FOR: (a) code/write approval — every code change, migration, deploy, before it runs; (b) a genuine product fork the governing law + the existing plan do NOT already answer. Everything else, Claude decides.
- BANNED ANTI-PATTERN: presenting an option-menu for a decision Claude should make. The ask-user-question tool is for product forks + preference elicitation, NOT for offloading sequencing Claude owns. "I don't care how, just do it right once" = Claude's cue to DECIDE and proceed, not to ask again.
- CONSULT THE PLAN BEFORE INVENTING: before proposing any "new" approach, check whether the plan ALREADY contains it (2026-06-23: a full Phase-4 breadth plan + LORAMER_COMPLETENESS_AUDIT_PLAN_V1 already existed; Claude began authoring a parallel one). If the plan has it, EXECUTE it and cite it; never freelance a duplicate.
- BLAST-RADIUS STATED, NOT ASSUMED: every proposed step names its blast radius, in order of safety — read-only investigation (always safe) → backend writer (isolated) → -next UI (preview-gated) → live-path (shared read-path / any live surface = STOP-and-confirm, never automatic). Live-path changes are allowed with graduated care. There is NO Meta review and no reviewer path — a shared-surface change STOP-and-confirms because of BLAST RADIUS (every client, every live surface), never a reviewer (the Meta App Review outcome that retired the reviewer path is owned by DECISIONS). Standing PRODUCTION obligations (not a freeze): the Meta data-deletion/deauth callback stays live (permanent Meta prod requirement), the Shopify install callback is a LIVE merchant path provisioning must not break, and Google Ads live snapshots stay quota-capped while Standard Access is pending (its live status owned by DECISIONS/QUEUE). A step touching a live/shared surface states "live-path" on its face and STOP-and-confirms.
- ONE THING AT A TIME: never stack a yes/no + a paste + a new question in one turn; when Russ answers a question, the NEXT turn does the WORK — it does not ask a follow-up question.
- OWN MISTAKES WITHOUT OFFLOADING: on error, acknowledge in one line, correct the route, and proceed — never convert recovery into more questions for Russ.

## C. GOVERNING LAW  (source: LORAMER_ESSENCE.md)
# ⛔ GOVERNING LAW — READ FIRST, OBEY ABSOLUTELY (no exceptions, no reinterpretation)
LoraMer captures EVERYTHING from EVERYWHERE and stores it FOREVER (until the customer cancels).
- EVERYTHING = every grain AND every dimension every platform exposes: account, campaign, ad_group/adset, ad, keyword, search_term, placement, device, geo, age/gender, hour/day, network, video, impression-share, all conversion/action types, product/variant, geo, and every other breakdown the API serves. If the API offers it, we capture it.
- EVERYWHERE = every connected platform: Google, Meta, Shopify, WooCommerce, GA — and any platform added later.
- FOREVER = forward capture appends daily and NOTHING is ever deleted or aged out; backfill reaches each platform's retention floor.
- FULL GRAIN, WITH HISTORY, IS THE DEFAULT. If the current code captures a thin slice (e.g. account-level only, or forward-only), that is INCOMPLETE CODE TO BE FINISHED — never a constraint to design around, and never labeled "future," "phase 4," "later," or "post-launch."
- The ONLY acceptable reason to not capture something is that the platform genuinely does not serve it (e.g. Google search terms beyond ~90d no longer exist to fetch). That is the ONLY exception. Filtering artifacts, missing params (campaign_id, time_increment), unbuilt writers, and "out of scope for this change" are NOT reasons — they are work to do.
- SEQUENCING is allowed ONLY as one-change-in-flight engineering discipline (each change reconciled, blast-radius-scoped, revert-ready). Sequencing NEVER means deferring, dropping, or relabeling any capture as out of scope. Everything is in scope, now, until Russ explicitly says otherwise.
- A fresh Claude that proposes a "persist the thin slice now, fuller grain later" plan has VIOLATED this law. Capture the full grain with history in the approved build.

## ⛔ PRE-ACTION GATE (forcing function — output this line before ANY Claude Code paste or ANY UI proposal; a skipped line is a visible violation)
`in-flight: [what's running / clear] · blast-radius: [read-only|backend-writer|-next-only|live-path] · grid: [which card, or N/A non-UI] · high-stakes claim: [none | VERIFIED/DERIVED/UNVERIFIED + what]`
(The gate field is BLAST-RADIUS. Levels, safest→widest: read-only (always safe) → backend-writer (isolated) → -next-only (preview-gated) → live-path (shared read-path / any live surface = STOP-and-confirm, never automatic). Live-path is ALLOWED with graduated care. There is NO Meta review, no reviewer path, no reviewer-driven hold — blast radius on a shared surface is about EVERY client and EVERY live surface, never a reviewer (the Meta App Review outcome that retired the reviewer path is owned by DECISIONS, not restated here). Standing PRODUCTION obligations (not a freeze, not a review): the Meta data-deletion/deauth callback must stay live (permanent Meta prod requirement), the Shopify install callback is a LIVE merchant path provisioning must not break, and Google Ads live snapshots stay quota-capped while Standard Access is pending — the 15k/day Basic cap binds route decisions; its live status lives in DECISIONS/QUEUE, never here.)
High-stakes = any claim gating a destructive/rotate/delete action, a "this is a bug" diagnosis, or a blast-radius/live-path judgment. Rationale: the rules already exist; this converts the ones most often dropped under momentum (one-in-flight, blast-radius, grid-native, claim-confidence) into required visible output so a skip is caught in the moment, not after. Root cause: 2026-07-01 session — repeated rule-breaks despite the rules being present; the failure was compliance, not coverage. Do not relitigate.

- MULTI-SOURCE METRIC PROVENANCE: Any metric that can differ by source (ROAS, conversions, CPA, revenue, attribution) surfaces EVERY source's value, each labeled with its origin and basis. Never hide, blend, collapse, or silently pick one. The store is source-of-truth only for explicit store-truth questions; each platform's value stands on its own for platform questions; offline-uploaded sales enable a valid ROAS even without online purchase. Lora holds all sources at once, distinguishes them, and explains WHY they differ (web-researching the explanation when it helps). Every metric, every surface.

A Gate-A that HAND-SUPPLIES the input which makes the flag fire is a REHEARSAL, not a proof. Gate-A must DERIVE its inputs from the real resolvers and real DB rows for the case under test. (7) "A6's timeout was the 8s ceiling / cron isn't 8s-bound" — both FALSE (A6 = missing partial index; cron IS 8s-bound). PATTERN: Claude wrote [PROVEN] on things it had DERIVED; Russ's insistence on verification caught all seven, not the resume protocol.
- [LAW] LIVE STATEMENT_TIMEOUT IS 8 SECONDS, NOT 120. supabaseAdmin → PostgREST connects as `authenticator` (statement_timeout=8s); role GUCs do NOT re-apply on SET ROLE, so 8s persists through service_role. The 120s cluster default is visible ONLY to MCP/superuser sessions — EXPLAIN ANALYZE in MCP measures against a limit real users NEVER get; any query benchmarked only via MCP is UNVERIFIED for production. Raising the ceiling is NOT the fix: every timeout found this session read millions of rows to return dozens; written correctly they run 30–72ms. The 8s limit is a SMOKE ALARM, and it protects the pooler — a slow query holds a connection, and a handful stall the whole app.
- [LAW] THERE IS NO STAGING DATABASE. An RPC/migration can only be proven where it is applied. CREATE OR REPLACE is the revert path. State this before every migration.

Vendor docs, SDK issue trackers, community repos, MCP servers, Stack Overflow. If a usable base exists, ADAPT IT rather than authoring from scratch. THE ADAPTATION IS NOT A SHORTCUT PAST VERIFICATION: found code is read IN FULL and Gate-A-proven exactly like code we wrote — never trusted on sight, never pasted on reputation. Found code is a STARTING POINT, never an answer. WHY: 2026-07-19, the Meta code-1/subcode-99 fix was fully engineered — split the call, batch by id, split-on-failure — and only THEN searched; the search confirmed 1/99 is a DOCUMENTED payload-size 500 whose standard remedy is exactly ID-batching, and surfaced meta-ads-mcp already carrying Meta creative error-handling to adapt from. The engineering was right, and it was still the expensive way to get there: hours of live probing to re-derive a published answer, with no cross-check against how anyone else solved it. Searching first is not a substitute for thinking — it is what stops us paying twice for the same knowledge. The trigger is now ANY build / fix / detector / schema change / diagnosis. THE ENFORCER is the VISIBLE THREE-FIELD HEADER on every such paste — HISTORY · WEB · REPO ([[SEARCH-BEFORE-NEW]] for history; this law for web; CHECK-FIRST for repo) — spelled out in LORAMER_HANDOFF's WEB-FIRST REPORT REQUIREMENT: a paste without it is MALFORMED and rejected on sight, before its content is read. HONEST LIMIT (stated, never implied away): the header's PRESENCE is inspectable — a paste either shows the three fields or it doesn't; whether a search TRULY preceded and informed the code is NOT inspectable by any guard (no check sees the temporal order of a search vs authorship). The header makes skipping VISIBLE; it cannot make searching HAPPEN — that stays discipline.
- [LAW] FIX-WITH-GUARD — A FIX IS NOT DONE UNTIL A MECHANICAL CHECK FAILS WHEN IT REGRESSES. The guard ships in the SAME commit as the fix; a fix without its guard is not shipped. PROSE IN A DOC IS NOT A GUARD. A guard must be SEEN TO FAIL against the pre-fix code — a guard never observed failing is not a guard, it is a comment. Build it on the CODE (the thing that cannot lie about itself), never on a doc (a doc can be honest-but-false). Guard the CLASS, not today's instance: if the next writer/page/field can reintroduce the bug and the check still passes, it guards nothing. Where a pattern lives in N files, do not guard the convention — COLLAPSE IT TO ONE SOURCE and guard that (the settleRevenue / META_BREADTH_FORWARD shape): we fix files, we do not enforce rules. PROOF THIS IS NEEDED, 2026-07-15, both from this repo: (1) ESSENCE has said capture EVERYTHING/EVERYWHERE/FOREVER since day one, and 10 Meta breadth dimensions froze at their ship dates anyway, with two cursors sealing backfill_complete=13/13 over a permanent hole — nothing failed, the cron returned 200, and ~1,900 rows/client/day silently stopped existing. The law was written, present, and read every session; it changed nothing because nothing MECHANICAL enforced it. (2) The 2026-06-20 client-context fix was real, correct, and shipped — and regressed three weeks later at team/page.tsx:22 because the pattern lived in six files and no check could see the seventh. A law you cannot fail is a wish. If a mechanical guard is genuinely not achievable for a rule, SAY SO plainly rather than shipping a check that manufactures false confidence — an unenforceable guard is worse than none: it reads green exactly like sync_state read backfill_complete=13/13 while forward capture did not exist.

## ⛔ DETERMINISM OF JUDGMENT [LAW]
Given the same question over the same finite data, Lora must reach the same conclusion 100% of the time. Her answer may change ONLY when new or different information exists — and when it does, she must be able to say WHY it changed (attribution, not silent drift). This holds across model upgrades: Opus 4.8 → 4.9 → any successor must not change a conclusion.
MECHANISM (the model is never the thing that decides):
- NUMBERS: computed in code via ONE canonical settle. Lora REPORTS, never derives. (Fix #1 Part B is the first brick.)
- RECOMMENDATIONS: derived from deterministic, versioned, testable RULES. The model's job is to EXPLAIN the rule's output in English, never to originate the judgment.
- PROSE: not bitwise stable and does not need to be. If literal repeatability is ever required: hash (question + data fingerprint + memory state + prompt version + corpus version + model ID) → return the cached answer.
- MEMORY is INSIDE the exception: a stored correction IS new information. Requirement is ATTRIBUTION — Lora must cite which correction changed her answer.
- MODEL UPGRADES are gated by the eval. No model reaches users until the golden set passes with CONCLUSIONS unchanged. Precedent: Sonnet→Opus flipped B1 and D2 with identical code; the eval caught both.

[VERIFIED 2026-07-14, Anthropic docs — platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/models/model-ids-and-versions + /overview]
- claude-opus-4-8 is a PINNED SNAPSHOT, not an alias. From the 4.6 generation on, the dateless ID IS the snapshot; Anthropic does not update weights or config under an existing ID. New versions ship under new IDs. LoraMer is correctly pinned.
- CAVEAT: weights are fixed per ID, but SERVING INFRASTRUCTURE (request router, safety classifiers, sampling logic) can change under the same ID. So bitwise output stability is NOT guaranteed even on a pinned ID. This is why determinism must live in code, not in the model.
- CAVEAT: every model ID has its own deprecation/retirement schedule. Migration is eventually mandatory; the eval gate is what makes it safe.
- CAVEAT: Opus 4.8 uses adaptive thinking with effort defaulting to HIGH on all surfaces — a live variance source today.

## ⛔ GROUNDED RECOMMENDATIONS / THE CORPUS [LAW]
- Lora's recommendations are grounded in a FROZEN, VERSIONED best-practice corpus — NEVER a live web search. Live search is a determinism bomb: same data, different day, different answer, no new information from the user. A corpus VERSION BUMP is new information: dated, attributable, eval-gated, roll-back-able.
- The corpus feeds the RULES, not the prose. Every recommendation CITES its source (corpus / the user's own correction / the platform's own doc). "Argue with her" is the product: she shows her sources, the user can dispute them. Same moat as multi-source metric provenance — explaining WHY, applied to judgment.
- POSITIONING (true, defensible): Google's and Meta's recommendation engines are structurally conflicted — their revenue IS the advertiser's spend. LoraMer's revenue is a subscription. Frame as INCENTIVE ASYMMETRY, never "they lie" — some of their advice is good, and overclaiming hands a skeptic an easy win.
- HONESTY BOUND: much of marketing canon is contested (brand vs. performance, attribution, incrementality). Never claim Lora knows THE truth. The claim is that she shows her sources and can be argued with.
- RISK FLAGGED, UNRESOLVED: ingesting third-party copyrighted marketing material into a corpus whose output is resold is real IP exposure, distinct from a human reading it. Public-domain / licensed / primary sources / Russ's own writing are clean. NOT legal advice — needs a real lawyer before the corpus scales.

## ⛔ "MAKE LORA YOUR OWN" — WHAT THE PROMISE CAN AND CANNOT CASH [LAW]
CAN: her name/voice/tone; the org's definitions (net sales, value model, ROAS basis); their documents; their retained corrections; their guardrails. All config, all real.
CANNOT: "a different mind" (weights are shared and rented); "she knows everything about us" (the librarian SELECTS — omission is the mechanism, not a bug); "exclusively yours" (direct tension with the cross-agency LoraMer tier — a POSITIONING FORK, unresolved: exclusive-yours vs compounding-across-agencies cannot both be headlined); "you own her" (implies portability/export — a moat hole if yes, marketing if no; decide knowingly).
COST LAW: per-org memory grows the briefing packet forever → input tokens rise per turn, per customer, permanently. Cost scales with ENGAGEMENT; best customers cost most. Must be budgeted before memory ships.
COPY GUARD: "she learns YOUR WAY OF WORKING" is true and defensible. "She BECOMES yours" is the check that bounces.

## HOMEPAGE / BOTTOM-OF-PAGE MESSAGE (banked for the two-door homepage item)
"The model is rented. The memory isn't." Every tool in the category runs on the same frontier AI anyone can rent — that's the starting line, not an edge. LoraMer's edge is what it KEEPS: every platform captured at full grain from the day you connect. So when Shopify and GA disagree, Lora shows BOTH, labeled by source, and explains why — the question every other tool hides from. A competitor can rent the same model tomorrow. They can't rent your history.
GUARDS: (a) "from the day you connect" is load-bearing — backfill is bounded by platform retention; never let it be edited into "all your history." (b) The cross-agency brain is ANONYMIZED PATTERNS, not data — any line implying Lora learns across agencies must read that way, or it reads as "your client data feeds your competitor."

If a change makes Lora more confident but not more provable, it is wrong.

## G. SETTLED-DECISIONS INDEX — do-not-relitigate, the complete map  (source: LORAMER_DECISIONS.md)
- GOVERNING LAW: capture EVERYTHING from EVERYWHERE, store FOREVER (until cancel), at FULL grain WITH history — now, not "later." Only exception = a platform genuinely doesn't serve the data. "Future/phase/scope" deferral of any capture = a VIOLATION. | LORAMER_ESSENCE.md (top) | do not relitigate.
- BEDROCK — TOTAL DATA CAPTURE: on connecting ANY source, auto-capture+backfill every grain/dimension/metric to the deepest the platform allows + forward daily. ENFORCEMENT: (1) auto-backfill on connect, (2) forward covers ALL grains, (3) completeness gate gates "onboarded", (4) fetched-but-unpersisted = DEFECT. No session may narrow this. | LORAMER_HANDOFF.md:35-43 | do not relitigate.
- AUTO-BACKFILL-ON-CONNECT design: on connect → cursor 'pending' (never run inline); cron sweep drains lap-by-lap; 20-lap safety cap; honors per-day self-reconcile HALT; never oscillate into a wall (L51); managed=full speed, Woo=extra-gentle. | CONTINUE_HERE (queued 2026-06-18), ROADMAP Data-Completeness-Onboarding | do not relitigate (design settled; build pending).
- RIGHT > FAST, always. Senior-engineer (20+yr) rigor. NO same mistake twice. | HANDOFF Operator-Level-Truth | do not relitigate.
- EVERYTHING IS LAUNCH-CRITICAL by default for July 14, 2026 — only exceptions: named next-phase (design then write) or external constraint (Meta/Google review). | HANDOFF GOSPEL, ESSENCE | do not relitigate.
- DESKTOP=MOBILE PARITY — ONE responsive app, verify both per increment, never fork. | HANDOFF GOSPEL + STANDING | do not relitigate.
PARITY — CANONICAL DEFINITION (2026-07-08, Russ): the BARE word "parity" ALWAYS means MACHINE PARITY — the iMac and the MacBook Air can perform ALL the same tasks, fully. A machine that cannot complete a build/push/deploy gate the other can is a MACHINE-PARITY VIOLATION to fix, not a footnote to route around. All OTHER parities MUST carry an explicit qualifier and are never written as bare "parity": "desktop=mobile visual coherence" (one responsive app, both surfaces coherent, never forked — cf. :18); "-next↔live capability parity" (the cutover flip gate — a FLIP surface is at parity when it shows the same data, correctly, as the current app; cf. LORAMER_NEXT_FLIP_PROGRAM.md); "connection-state probe parity" (all 5 platforms probed equivalently); and misc technical axes (quota parity, forward/backfill parity, writer-behavior parity) which stay locally qualified at their use site. Rule: if a doc says "parity" with no qualifier, it means MACHINE parity; every non-machine sense REQUIRES its qualifier. | LORAMER_PARITY_GLOSSARY, 2026-07-08 | do not relitigate.
- The MOAT is the intelligence/memory/recommendations layer, NOT the dashboard. Goal = real-world recommendations & growth, not data display. | HANDOFF North-Star, ROADMAP Strategy | do not relitigate.
SEARCH-BEFORE-FORKING (2026-07-08, Russ): Before presenting ANY principle, default, product direction, or governing rule as if it needs deciding — i.e. before offering Russ a fork/option-menu on something that sounds like product law — Claude MUST first search past chats (conversation_search) and the repo docs for a prior decision. If it's already decided, CITE it and build to it; do NOT re-offer it as open. The tell that triggers this: Claude catching itself about to say "your call / here are the options" on anything resembling a settled principle. Re-litigating closed decisions is the single biggest waste of Russ's time. This session alone, mobile-first-standard, store-truth ROAS law, and "both ROAS labeled" were all already banked and got mistakenly re-opened. Searching first turns "let me propose this" into "you already decided this, building to it." | LORAMER_SEARCH_BEFORE_FORKING, 2026-07-08 | do not relitigate.
TWO-SOURCE VERIFY (2026-07-09, Russ): the relitigate/settled gate requires BOTH stores — the repo docs AND past chats (conversation_search). A single-store check is NOT "verified": searching the repo alone and finding nothing does NOT license "this is new/unsettled" (and vice-versa) — a decision can live in either store, so a one-store miss produces false "this is new" conclusions, the exact way settled calls get re-opened. Repo-only = still UNVERIFIED. Applies to SEARCH-BEFORE-NEW and SEARCH-BEFORE-FORKING alike: check both, or the gate did not pass. | LORAMER_TWO_SOURCE_VERIFY, 2026-07-09 | do not relitigate.
CONTRADICTION STOP (2026-07-09, Russ): when an instruction contradicts a banked GOVERNING LAW / ESSENCE / settled DECISION, Claude Code HALTS — it does NOT execute it, does NOT silently comply, and does NOT quietly "improve" it into compliance. It flags the contradiction in ONE line WITH the citation (which banked rule, and where), states the conflict plainly, and waits. The banked law wins over an in-the-moment instruction UNLESS Russ explicitly overrides it AS an override (knowingly changing the law) — not by accident. Executing a law-contradicting instruction is a failure even when the instruction came from Russ; the correct move is halt-flag-cite-wait, then let Russ confirm-as-override or restate. | LORAMER_CONTRADICTION_STOP, 2026-07-09 | do not relitigate.

Window label = the ACTUAL captured omni_purchase span, not the nominal edge (action_type back-drain lag). tsc + npm run build GREEN. - [LESSON 2026-07-15 — why the original audit undercounted 3→6] A PARTIAL grain filter is an INDEX MISS THAT LOOKS CORRECT. The 8s audit grepped for latest-date queries with NO grain filter and found 3; it missed ga-overview (entity_level only), money and store-detect (missing breakdown_value) because they LOOK filtered. A partial index is all-or-nothing: the planner must prove the query implies the FULL predicate (all three columns) or the index is silently unusable. When auditing partial-index coverage, grep for the sites that DON'T match the predicate EXACTLY — not for the ones with no filter at all. | LORAMER_LATEST_DATE_ACCOUNT_GRAIN_V1, 2026-07-15 | do not relitigate.

- [LESSON 2026-07-16, RUSS'S FRAMING — banked at HIS instruction because the error was his and the refutation was code] **(a) THE CURSOR "UNSEAL" NEVER EXISTED.** The G1(b) spec called for a sync_state cursor write to "unseal" meta_device/meta_video (backfill_complete=13/13) before the hole could be filled. It was asserted for hours, carried forward from a prior-session summary, and it was FALSE: the 6 writers touch sync_state ZERO times and the 6 thin routes touch it zero times in CODE — they are STATELESS and take explicit startDate/endDate (api/backfill/meta-device/route.ts:4 says so in its own header). backfill_complete gates EXACTLY ONE line — drain-registry.ts:155, the DRAIN's rangeLap — and nothing on the fill path. The fill needed no cursor write and no new code; it was 78 invocations of routes that already existed. **(b) 33.2s/client/DAY DOES NOT EXTRAPOLATE.** The spec predicted 17 days × 33.2s ≈ 564s/client, "far past the 680s budget", and specced a cron/paging plan around it. MEASURED: the whole 16-day hole cost **98s/client** — because the writers use monthChunks(), so a 16-day range is ONE chunk costing the same CALL COUNT as one day (time_increment=1 returns every day in one report), just more rows. ~3× one day, not 17×. The cron/paging plan was solving a problem that did not exist. **THE RULE: MEASURE, DON'T REASON — AND CHALLENGE THE FRAMING EVERY TIME, INCLUDING RUSS'S.** A spec handed down with confidence is still a hypothesis; both of these were refuted in minutes by reading code and running one dryRun. Inheriting a framing is how a prior session's guess becomes this session's fact — the exact mechanism behind G3 (a doc attesting a FETCH, read as persistence) and G1 (sync_state reading complete over a hole that had no forward writer). | LORAMER_META_BREADTH_HOLE_FILL, 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.

- [LESSON 2026-07-16 — RUSS'S, banked at his instruction] **READ THE ARTIFACT, NOT THE SCREENSHOT.** Two hypotheses were built off a screenshot of the rejection + a visual read of the page, and both were wrong in ways the actual bytes settled in minutes: the page already said "LoraMer" 11 times, and the split spans had no whitespace. A screenshot shows what a human SAW; the artifact shows what a machine READS — and the objection came from a machine-or-reviewer reading the artifact. Same family as the parity-audit law (an INVENTORY question cannot answer a CORRECTNESS question) and as G3 (a doc attesting a FETCH, read as persistence): each is a claim about a thing, mistaken for the thing. The fix is always the same — go get the bytes. | LORAMER_LANDING_H1_APPNAME_V1, 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.

- [DECIDED 2026-07-16] **iOS FLEX MIN-WIDTH: A SCROLLER IS NOT ENOUGH.** `.md table` already carried overflow-x:auto — the scroll container was correct and in the right place the whole time. It was overruled because .bubbleAssistant, .scroll and .panel computed min-width:auto as flex items, so the table's intrinsic nowrap width propagated up the chain to .panel. .inputBar is a SIBLING of .scroll inside .panel, so a widened panel carries the send arrow off-screen with it — the arrow was never mis-placed, the panel was panned. ANY scroll container inside a flex chain needs min-width:0 on EVERY flex ancestor or it does not fire on iOS. Headless WebKit does NOT reproduce this — it honors the scroller. A headless render measurement is NOT evidence about this class of defect. Assert the CSS INVARIANT (min-width:0 present on the flex chain; overflow-x:auto on .md table and .md pre), or test on a real device. Do not build a guard whose green means nothing. | 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.
- [DECIDED 2026-07-16] **REACT STATE LAGS INSIDE A SYNCHRONOUS CALLBACK.** react-grid-layout 1.5.3 fires onBreakpointChange THEN onLayoutChange in the SAME synchronous call stack (lib source ResponsiveReactGridLayout.onWidthChange, lines 174→175). A guard reading React STATE set by the first callback is STALE inside the second — setState batches/defers, refs do not. CardGrid's `isMobile` guard read the lagging `bp` state, so entering the sm breakpoint wrote the sm w:1 `stacked` layout into the DESKTOP layout, and autosave persisted it → the dashboard-cards-collapse-to-70px bug. FIX (LORAMER_NEXT_GRID_BP_REF_V1): read a bpRef.current set synchronously in onBreakpointChange. RULE: any guard inside a synchronous callback chain must read a REF, not state. Introduced 2026-07-09 (bfe1a3d), live one week, found 2026-07-16 by Russ noticing collapsed cards. | do not relitigate.

DETECT-AT-LOAD heals them: discard a desktop layout matching the corrupt signature (all items w:1 AND all x:0, ≥2 items) and fall back to the PAGE's own default (storeDefaultView on store pages, else defaultOverviewView) — no DB write, no migration, reversible, heals on next visit; the next autosave persists it. - [DECIDED 2026-07-16] **dvh DOES NOT SHRINK FOR THE KEYBOARD.** dvh/svh/lvh account for the browser's retractable ADDRESS BAR, NOT the on-screen keyboard. Nothing in the codebase listened to window.visualViewport — zero grep hits. BIND a fixed sheet to visualViewport (height + offsetTop) or it never knows where the screen is. A comment that describes INTENT instead of BEHAVIOR is worse than no comment: it stops the next reader from looking. Facts that are true AND load-bearing-sounding are the most dangerous input to a build, because they survive every check except the one that matters: does the bug go away. Ship a fix ONLY when the failure has been REPRODUCED and the fix is proven against that reproduction. ★UI-OVERFLOW was never reproduced outside Russ's phone, and four theories in a row were wrong — each internally coherent, each derived from a CSS read + an engine that does not reproduce the bug. | 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.
- [LESSON 2026-07-16] **POSTGREST 1000-ROW CAP.** Any PostgREST RPC that returns SETOF (a table / rows) is SILENTLY capped at 1000 rows AND returned UNORDERED — no error, no warning. An aggregation that can exceed 1000 groups MUST return a SINGLE jsonb array (the groups live inside one scalar row, where the row cap cannot bite). Caught in G2 2A Gate-A by a distinct-count mismatch (1000 vs 3169): a top-N over a silently-truncated, unordered set is a WRONG ANSWER THAT LOOKS RIGHT. The SETOF→jsonb return-type change needs DROP+CREATE, not CREATE OR REPLACE. | LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_SQL_AGG_V1, 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION 2026-07-16] **SQL-SIDE BREAKDOWN AGGREGATION = the scoped RPC `query_breakdown_agg`** (migration 038): a client-anchored GROUP BY on idx_metrics_daily_client_platform_bt_level_date, returning a jsonb array of (breakdown_value, parent_entity_id, sums); the JS layer keeps entity-level scope + canonicalize + sort + value-ASC tiebreak + topN + notes, so it stays byte-identical to the cent. PostgREST `db-aggregates-enabled` was REJECTED — it opens aggregate surface on EVERY table for EVERY caller to solve one query. Do NOT raise again. | LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_SQL_AGG_V1, 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.

An 8s statement_timeout check ALONE would have waved this through. MEASURE THE PAYLOAD, not just execution time. Fix: a bounded topN RPC (migration 039) does ORDER BY + LIMIT in SQL → 4.5 KB. | LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_BOUNDED_TOPN_V1, 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION 2026-07-16] **BOUNDED-RPC SCOPE.** bounded = a NEW tool type (geo / ga_* / age_gender) OR an existing type at an EXPLICITLY-requested entityLevel (Google device/hour at ad_group/keyword). The 14 EXISTING tool types at their DEFAULT level keep the all-groups path, preserving byte-identical (proven, 6 pairs). The bounded branch THROWS if canonicalize is set — canonicalization collapses values AFTER aggregation, so a SQL topN taken BEFORE the collapse ranks the wrong things. Never silently degrade. | LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_BOUNDED_TOPN_V1, 2026-07-16 | do not relitigate.

Live-DB reachability (is every CAPTURED tuple declared?) stays the SEPARATE non-build script (scripts/breakdown-reachability-check.mjs) — CI has no network. - [LAW, 2026-07-17] **THE FRESHNESS GATE PROVES THE DIGEST MATCHES ITS SOURCES. IT DOES NOT PROVE THE SOURCES AGREE WITH EACH OTHER.** On 2026-07-17 the session opened with a stale RUSS ACTION #1 telling Russ to go read a Google OAuth rejection surface for a gate closed the day before. CONTINUE_HERE:580 said OPEN. DECISIONS:443 said CLOSED. Every hash was green, 10/10, the whole time. ROOT CAUSE: the 07-16 OAuth-closure paste wrote the ledger and regenerated the digest but never updated CONTINUE_HERE, which is what §E reads from. The gate could not catch it because it is a consistency check, not a truth check. RULE: when a decision CLOSES something, update EVERY doc that asserts it is open — the ledger AND the resume path. Grep the closed thing by name across all gated source docs before the wrap. The ledger is authoritative when docs conflict, but a conflict that reaches a session opener has already cost the session. THIS IS THE THIRD TIME IN TWO DAYS: SECURITY_POSTURE 7.9 claimed the reviewer login was dead infra (wrong, and would have talked a session into deleting live Shopify compliance); claude-tools.ts:118 described entityLevel behavior the code did not have; chat.module.css:3 claimed 100dvh shrinks for the keyboard. A green check that answers a narrower question than the reader assumes is the recurring defect in this project. FOURTH INSTANCE, same day: the digest's §H open-queue index sliced the QUEUE at a "DONE — DO NOT REBUILD" marker and silently omitted EVERY item banked after 2026-07-15 — the entire G2 arc's follow-ons, ★UI-OVERFLOW, ★SUPPORT-BOT, and the rest. The items were in the QUEUE file the whole time and every hash was green. A condensed index is a CLAIM about completeness. If it cannot defend that claim, it must not be presented as the open list. Fixed 2026-07-17 (LORAMER_DIGEST_H_COMPLETENESS_V1: §H now selects OPEN items by meaning — block-grouped, terminal-status, position-independent — with only the fenced DONE appendix skipped; proven by a deliberate below-marker append that the old slice dropped and the new extractor catches). ⚠ STILL UNFIXED, SAME CLASS, NAMED NOT FIXED: §C's honesty-clause selector in build-resume-digest.mjs takes essence.split('\\n').filter(l=>l.trim()).slice(-1)[0] — the LAST non-empty line of ESSENCE by POSITION; append anything after the honesty clause and the digest silently swaps in the wrong line. (The header/fence extractors fail LOUD via must(); the slice(-1) fails SILENT, like §H did.) | LORAMER_SOURCE_CONFLICT_GATE_V1 + LORAMER_DIGEST_H_COMPLETENESS_V1, 2026-07-17 | do not relitigate.
- [LAW, 2026-07-17] **NEVER WRITE DOWN A FACT ANOTHER DOC OWNS. POINT AT THE OWNER.** 2026-07-17 recon: ~10 volatile facts were hand-copied into ~27 live places across ESSENCE, HANDOFF, SECURITY_POSTURE and CONTINUE_HERE §E. Five of the seven silent-drift defects of 07-16/17 lived in ONE class — external approval status (Meta/Shopify/OAuth/Standard-Access). The doc set is NOT overbuilt; each doc has a unique authoritative core (ESSENCE=law · DECISIONS=status/ledger · QUEUE=backlog · CONTINUE_HERE=narrative+opener · HANDOFF=manual · SECURITY_POSTURE=security · REGISTRY=dimensions · RESUME_INSTRUCTIONS=resume-wording). The COPIES are the disease. THE LESSON FROM THE REGISTRY (2026-07-16): prevention beats detection. Two files that had to agree by hand did not, and the fix was not a better checker — it was ONE declared source with the rest generated; drift became IMPOSSIBLE instead of detectable. Apply the same shape to docs: one owner per fact, everyone else POINTS. A STATUS DOC IS A FUTURE LIE WITH A TIMESTAMP — an AI will happily write one; it looks like diligence; every hand-copied status is a lie waiting for the source to move. WHAT CANNOT BE COLLAPSED: "what is next" is a JUDGMENT — the QUEUE has no priority field and ordering is explicitly Russ's call, re-decided per session; it stays AUTHORED and gets a CONTRADICTION CHECK, not generation (★SOURCE-CONFLICT-GATE must verify §E's named next-item still reads open in the QUEUE). And session NARRATIVE is irreducible authored history — a generator would omit or fabricate it, so it is written by hand and left as dated snapshots (frozen history is NOT drift; only LIVE assertions drift). WHAT SHIPPED 2026-07-17 (LORAMER_DOCS_SINGLE_OWNER_V1, the DELETION half — the board + generated-§E + the gate stay QUEUED as the post-launch completion, deliberately NOT built five days from launch): deleted/pointered ~the live copies in ESSENCE (2), HANDOFF (4 + the dead June-3 state block, ~96 lines), SECURITY_POSTURE (5), CONTINUE_HERE §E (5); corrected the stale "NEXT BUILD = G2" opener (G2 closed 07-16); retired the self-admittedly-stale INVENTORY from the gated set. | LORAMER_DOCS_SINGLE_OWNER_V1, 2026-07-17 | do not relitigate.
- [POINTER, 2026-07-17] **EXECUTABLE RULES LIVE IN CLAUDE.md, NOT HERE.** The ONE-CHANGE-IN-FLIGHT rule was banked as prose in HANDOFF/ESSENCE/DECISIONS and broken FOUR times in two days — because DECISIONS is where the STRATEGY Claude reads a rule, and reading it there cannot bind the EXECUTOR (Claude Code) who receives the mid-flight paste. Banking a repeat-offense rule as more prose is the FAILURE MODE, not the fix. It now lives as a REFUSAL gate in CLAUDE.md (LORAMER_CLAUDE_MD_INFLIGHT_GATE_V1), which the Claude Code harness auto-loads every session with no paste — the one place it can actually fire. RULE-HOME LAW: a rule broken more than once needs an ENFORCER, not another entry; it belongs in CLAUDE.md (binds Claude Code) or a build guard (binds the compiler), never in a doc that is only read. | LORAMER_CLAUDE_MD_INFLIGHT_GATE_V1, 2026-07-17 | do not relitigate.
- [LESSON, 2026-07-17] **THE ANTI-COPY GATES FOUND THEIR FIRST VICTIM IN THEIR OWN FILE.** 2026-07-17: while adding the ownership gates to CLAUDE.md, Claude Code found CLAUDE.md:37 asserting the chat model is claude-sonnet-4-6 while the code read claude-opus-4-8 (Opus floor = law, G10). A copied code-fact, drifted to the wrong value, in the file that now forbids copied facts. THIS EXACT DRIFT ALREADY COST US ONCE: on 2026-07-16 prod Lora was silently running Sonnet while the 28/28 eval measured local Opus. Same fact, same disease, different surface. THE FIX IS NOT THE VALUE. Correcting the value leaves a copy that drifts again. CODE OWNS CODE FACTS. A doc may POINT at where a value lives; it may not STATE the value. "Correct today" is what every one of the 27 copies was on the day it was written. Fixed by pointing: CLAUDE.md now names LORA_CHAT_MODEL + src/app/api/chat/route.ts + src/app/api/insight/route.ts as the owners, and asserts no model value. - [DECISION, 2026-07-17] **ONE LORA, ONE MODEL.** Banked 2026-07-14 as OPUS 4.8 EVERYWHERE; the reasoning is recorded here because it was argued and settled. THE CAPABILITY FLOOR (2026-05-23): the insight banner IGNORED a client directive. Escential's profile said "Ignore ROAS as a primary performance metric for this account." Extraction worked, user_notes carried it, the code was correct — Haiku ignored it anyway, because in 50-word mode it could not follow a negative instruction while the ROAS number was the most flag-worthy thing on screen. THE MODEL WAS THE LIMIT. A banner that violates a client's declared value_model is confidently wrong on the first surface any user sees — the same failure class as the Display hour-0 midnight bid-down. value_model is law; a model that cannot honor it makes the law decorative. THE CONTINUITY LAW (Russ, 2026-07-17) — the stronger argument: Project 14 banks ONE LORA per client across all surfaces, picking up where the user left off. One mind cannot be three models. If banner output enters conversation history that chat later reads, the quality floor of the whole conversation becomes the WORST model that ever wrote into it — a shallow read poisoning a deep one, permanently, in the store. The 05-23 failure was not a banner bug; it was one mind having a bad thought and writing it down. ROUTE-BY-DIFFICULTY IS THE RIGHT ANSWER TO A QUESTION WE ARE NOT ASKING. Claude argued it on 2026-07-14 — Opus for chat, Haiku for the blurb, standard practice. It assumes surfaces are independent. Ours are not. That pushback was WRONG and is recorded as wrong. COST IS NOT THE ARGUMENT. Measured: $10.16 across every client over six weeks; heaviest user $4.10. Opus 4.8 at $5/$25 is ~1.67x Sonnet. Cost has never been the gate. Capability and continuity are. | LORAMER_ONE_LORA_ONE_MODEL_V1, 2026-07-17 | do not relitigate.

- [LAW, 2026-07-17] **EVERY AUDIT SO FAR COMPARED US TO OURSELVES.** Three capture audits passed while Shopify, Woo and GA hour grain was being discarded at the writer. They asked "do we capture Shopify?" — yes — and never asked "what does Shopify's API OFFER, and what fraction do we store?" The reference was always our own inventory, registry, or writers. A writer that stores 40% of what an API serves passes every audit whose reference is that writer. G2 (07-16) found 118 tuples captured and unreachable — the READ side of this same disease; the WRITE side was never checked. THE ONLY AUDIT THAT ANSWERS IT enumerates what each platform's API OFFERS — from the platform's own published docs, not from our code — and diffs that against metrics_daily. Any audit whose reference is our own code cannot find what we never asked the API for. - [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-22] **LORAMER_QUERY_COMPLETENESS_V1 — query_metrics stops handing Lora a silently-incomplete total (T0 #2 SEV-1, shipped).** THE T0#2 RECON ranked the false-zero surface: the RENDER (useCardData→CardViz "Couldn't load"/"No data") and the QUERY-ERROR path (metrics-query throws → tool loop → {error}) are ALREADY honest; the real danger is a SUCCESSFUL read of INCOMPLETE data — a total that silently omits a platform whose capture FAILED (Shelley's woo host-500), presented as a whole number, which Lora would state as fact. coverage.state MISSES it (it reads 'covered' for a window that starts in-capture but ends past the last captured day). "annotates with coverage but NOT annotateContribution — a total can be emitted without the flag") → PASS after; wired into `npm run build` (9 guards). (2) GATE-A dry, ZERO writes, 10/10 on Shelley (23c697bb) real + synthetic-degraded: her recent window flags trailing_gap (her real state today); a degraded window flags capture_failing DESPITE coverage='covered' (the SEV-1 miss caught); a healthy client + honest-absence states (predates/not_connected) do NOT false-alarm. tsc 0, build 0. - [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-22] **LORAMER_QUERY_COMPLETENESS_V1 SLICE 2 — the incomplete-total flag reaches the -next cards + the substitution is labeled + the residual is closed (T0 #2 SEV-2, shipped).** Extends SEV-1 (which flagged Lora only) to the SCREEN: annotateContribution is wired into client-metrics (full getCoverageForWindows) + portfolio-metrics (annotateContributionBatch — ONE platform_connections query for all clients; full per-client coverage would be N heavy reconciles) + card-roas + store-stats. THREE decisions banked: (1) SUBSTITUTION LABEL — settleRevenue precedence store>ga silently falls to GA when store rows are absent; substitutedStorePlatform() detects a FAILURE-caused fallback (revenueSource='ga' AND the store is capture_failing/trailing_gap) and labels it ("Revenue shown is GA — the <store> total is missing, its capture is failing, not $0"); LEAST BLAST RADIUS = computed at the CALLER from revenueSource + contribution, the canonical settleRevenue stays an untouched pure numeric fold (a failure context does not belong inside the settle). (2) RESIDUAL CLOSED — coverage.ts exposes lastCaptured (last captured day) on CoverageResult, and the failing-window test keys on it (window.endDate > lastCaptured while failing), not only first_failure_at — closing the sliver where data stopped (07-19) before the streak clock started (07-23). VERIFIED: FIX-WITH-GUARD check-query-completeness.mjs EXTENDED + seen-to-fail on the reverted routes/render (client-metrics/portfolio-metrics/MerView/useCardData, then card-roas/store-stats/CardViz) → PASS; wired in npm run build (9 guards). - [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-22] **LORAMER_QUERY_COMPLETENESS_V1 SLICE 3 — the live defect: a stale tail rendered CLEAN because the tail test was gated behind the health streak; + money/ga-overview were never wired (T0 #2, shipped).** WHY SLICE 2 STILL LEAKED: on Russ's phone Shelley's ROAS + money cards showed NO amber caption though her woo was captured only through 07-19 in a window ending 07-21. ROOT CAUSE — slice 2's failing-window test (`failing = streakActive && (endDate > lastCaptured || …)`) keyed the lastCaptured comparison on `streakActive`; Shelley's woo was HEALTHY (host-500 streak not yet recorded), so `failing=false` and the total rendered whole. A green harness said otherwise because it hand-supplied `state:'trailing_gap'`/synthetic-degraded coverage — it never drove the REAL getCoverageForWindows, which returns `state:'covered', lastCaptured:'2026-07-19'` for that window (the classic green-check-answers-a-narrower-question). THE FIX (query-completeness.ts): a NEW `stale_tail` ContributionStatus, computed INDEPENDENT of any streak — `staleTail = lastCaptured != null && daysBetween(lastCaptured, endDate) >= LAG_TOLERANCE_DAYS`, evaluated AFTER capture_failing (a KNOWN failing connection still wins) and BEFORE trailing_gap. Added to the INCOMPLETE set + noteSet + substitutedStorePlatform + buildIncompleteNote. THREE decisions: (1) LAG_TOLERANCE_DAYS = 2 with `>=` (gap of exactly 2 days flags). Russ's instruction said "tolerance 2 + more than"; a literal `> 2` (gap ≥ 3) would leave Shelley's gap-of-exactly-2 UNFLAGGED — i.e. would NOT fix the reported defect — so `>= 2` was chosen and is FLAGGED for Russ to correct if he truly meant ≥ 3. 1 day is normal fleet capture lag and must not false-alarm (proved on a real fresh client). (2) MONEY + GA-OVERVIEW WIRED — the two TOTAL surfaces slice 2 missed. money/route.ts (MoneyWaterfall) + ga-overview/route.ts (GaOverview) now build `incompleteNote` via the same buildIncompleteNote and render the amber caption. Route-by-route audit of all 12 /api/next routes: WIRED (6 headline totals) = client-metrics · portfolio-metrics · card-roas · store-stats · money · ga-overview; N/A (6) = card-breakdown + entities (per-slice/per-entity DECOMPOSITIONS, not a single headline number, and each carries its own capture `note`) · client-timeseries + store-timeseries (per-day CHARTS — a stale tail shows as the line ending early / missing recent points, self-evident, not a false whole number) · clients (portfolio list) · layouts (saved-view config). (3) buildIncompleteNote now SPLITS wording — "capture is failing" (capture_failing/trailing_gap, a known-broken connection) vs "capture is behind — its most recent days aren't captured yet" (stale_tail, merely lagging); wording them the same asserted a false thing about a healthy-but-lagging connection (prompt-honesty). VERIFIED: (a) guard EXTENDED with a BEHAVIORAL check — it transpiles+runs computeContribution over the exact Shelley case (healthy, no streak, covered, lastCaptured 2 days back) and FAILS the build unless it returns 'stale_tail'; SEEN-TO-FAIL on pre-slice-3 code (reported "returns 'ok', not 'stale_tail' — gated behind the health signal") → PASS after; also asserts money/ga-overview/MoneyWaterfall attach+render the note. (b) GATE-A REBUILT HONESTLY — a transpile-on-require harness drives the REAL getCoverageForWindows + REAL readHealthForClients against prod (read-only), 4/4: (a) Shelley 05-02..07-21 → REAL coverage state='covered' lastCaptured='2026-07-19' → stale_tail + caption names WooCommerce; (b) window ending 07-20 (1-day gap) → clean, no caption; (c) a REAL fresh healthy client (Meta, maxCaptured 07-21) → clean (no false alarm on normal lag); (d) Shelley REAL coverage + one SYNTHETIC live streak → capture_failing (streak wins over stale_tail). PROVENANCE STATED: a/b/c fully real; d's health row is synthetic because a live failure streak cannot be created in prod without a write. tsc 0, build 0, npm run guard all-green. STILL OPEN (Gate-B, not blocking): the on-device mobile (sm) render of these captions is unproven on the phone. | LORAMER_QUERY_COMPLETENESS_V1, 2026-07-22 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-23] **LORAMER_QUERY_COMPLETENESS_V1 SLICE 4 — the caption is PER-METRIC (a store stale tail no longer captions Spend) + the ROAS caption wraps instead of truncating (T0 #2, shipped).** DEFECT 1 (a FALSE statement, live on Shelley): client-metrics built ONE client-wide `incompleteNote` over ALL platforms (`getCoverageForWindows(clientId, [], win)`) and every stat card read that same note (useCardData:90), so her woo stale tail captioned Spend ($2,836) AND Conversions (50) — metrics WooCommerce feeds NEITHER. THE FIX — **metricContributors(metric) is the SINGLE metric→platform map** (query-completeness.ts), DERIVED from client-metrics' own aggregation (NO existing map existed — ADS_PLATFORMS/STORE_PLATFORMS were duplicated across 5+ files, none exported as a metric mapping; `platformsForToolType` is for breakdown tool-types, not metrics): ads (google/meta) → spend/clicks/impressions/conversions/conversionValue/ctr/cpc/cpa · store (shopify/woo) + ga → revenue · store → orders/aov/money · roas/mer → both classes · ga → sessions. `buildIncompleteNote` gains a 3rd `metric` arg that filters the contribution to that metric's contributors BEFORE wording; client-metrics returns a per-metric `incompleteNotes` map; each stat card reads its OWN metric's note (useCardData `d.incompleteNotes[m]`, never the client-wide one). MerView KEEPS the client-wide `incompleteNote` (MER = revenue ÷ ad spend is fed by every platform class, so client-wide is correct there). card-roas/store-stats/money/ga-overview unchanged — each already scopes coverage to the right platform set. DEFECT 2 (ROAS caption truncated at the card edge while stat cards wrapped): `.partial` already wrapped (`overflow-wrap:anywhere`); the CONTAINER didn't — `.roasBody { overflow:auto }` made it a HORIZONTAL scroll region, so the stretched flex caption sized to the widest row's scroll-width (not the visible card width) and `overflow-wrap` never engaged. FIX — `.roasBody` → `overflow-x:hidden; overflow-y:auto` (matching `.card`/`.statBody`); `.roasRow` `min-width:0`. - [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-23] **LORAMER_QUERY_COMPLETENESS_V1 SLICE 5 — stale_tail measures against the CAPTURABLE FRONTIER, not the raw window end (kills the nightly healthy-client false alarm; T0 #2, shipped).** DEFECT: stale_tail compared lastCaptured to the window's endDate; when the window ends TODAY, today is not yet capturable (the forward cron runs 08:00-10:59 UTC), so EVERY healthy client tripped amber during the nightly pre-window gap (~00:00-08:00 UTC = 8pm-4am ET) until the window advanced the frontier — a caption that fires on healthy clients trains the user to ignore it. (Surfaced by slice-4's Gate-A observation + the 07-23 capture investigation: the whole fleet sits at 07-21 pre-window BY DESIGN, not failure — utc_now was 04:29, before the 08:00 window; zero streaks; meta/ga fully healthy.) FIX (query-completeness.ts): `capturableFrontier(now)` = the last day the forward cron is EXPECTED to have captured — UTCtoday−1 if today's window has finished (now past FORWARD_WINDOW_END_HOUR_UTC), else UTCtoday−2. DERIVATION, STATED: the cron (/api/cron/sync) captures resolveDateWindow('YESTERDAY') (sync/route.ts:161); its window is 04:00-06:59 ET so the ET date == the UTC date → captureDate = (run's UTC day)−1 in EST and EDT alike (confirmed live: the 07-22 08:18 UTC run wrote 07-21). computeContribution clamps the stale comparison end to min(window.endDate, frontier); a past-ending window is unaffected (endDate < frontier → measures the raw end). `now` is injected (default new Date()) so it is testable and the routes pass the real clock. LAG_TOLERANCE_DAYS stays 2, now measured FROM THE FRONTIER. `FORWARD_WINDOW_END_HOUR_UTC=11` is NOT a free hardcode — the guard RE-DERIVES it from vercel.json's forward schedule (max hour + 1) and fails the build on mismatch, so the rule follows the schedule if it moves (the "read the cron config" requirement, enforced mechanically). VERIFIED: (a) guard EXTENDED — behavioral seen-to-fail at now=2026-07-23T04:29Z (pre-window): a HEALTHY client captured through the frontier (07-21) on a TODAY-ending window must NOT be stale_tail (pre-fix measured raw end 07-23 → gap 2 → stale → build fails), and a client 2 behind the frontier (07-19) MUST be; + static — the vercel.json re-derivation (independently computed hour 10+1=11, matching the constant). (b) GATE-A on REAL inputs (getCoverageForWindows + readHealthForClients vs prod; only `now` and case-c's streak injected + named) 5/5: PRE-window (04:29Z, frontier 07-21) a real healthy client (google/meta at 07-21) NOT captioned + Shelley woo (07-19) STILL captioned; POST-window (12:00Z, frontier 07-22) both still correct; Shelley + a synthetic live streak → capture_failing still WINS over stale_tail. tsc 0, guard all-green, build 0. VERIFIED mechanism (read this session, not asserted): src/app/api/intelligence/route.ts:30 gates whether `connectionHealth` is populated onto the intelligence payload on this flag, and src/lib/intelligence/build-claude-context.ts:1125-1137 renders the connection-health honesty block ONLY when that array is present — so with the flag OFF the block never reached Lora's prompt (intelligence-types.ts:664 states it outright: "absent → no honesty block in the prompt"). CONSEQUENCE: the flag gated NOT ONLY the new 'degraded' block but the PRE-EXISTING 'reconnect' warning (LORAMER_CONNECTION_HEALTH_V1) — so for as long as it was OFF, Lora could never tell a user that a connection's LOGIN had died (reconnect) OR that capture was persistently failing (degraded). A dead credential was invisible to the one surface whose job is to name it. THE CLASS: a flag that defaults a truth-telling surface OFF is the same failure as connection-health masking a persistent 500 — both leave a real, knowable problem unspoken; the flag just masks it one config layer up. Now ON in production (Production ="1", verified via `vercel env pull` 2026-07-22; Preview still unset — affects PR previews only, not the deploy target). DURABLE GUARD (NOT built, filed): a truth-telling surface (honesty block, reconnect/degraded notice, any "we can't see X" warning) must not sit behind a default-OFF flag; if flagged at all it defaults ON and the flag only SUPPRESSES for a named reason — the same "masking needs an enforcer, not a note" logic that [[LORAMER_CONN_FAILURE_STREAK_V1]] applied to the streak. | LORAMER_HEALTH_UI_FLAG_MUTED_LORA_V1, 2026-07-23 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-23] **LORAMER_ACCOUNT_ROW_INVARIANT_V1 — the account-row-per-day invariant is now a live-DB detector, guard-enforced (T0 #4, shipped).** THE INVARIANT (uniform, no family exemption): for every (client_id, platform, date) with ANY row in metrics_daily, a row with entity_level='account' AND breakdown_type='' AND breakdown_value='' must exist for that same (client_id, platform, date). It is the schema-UNENFORCED assumption the six latest-date reads rest on ([[LORAMER_LATEST_DATE_ACCOUNT_GRAIN_V1]]: money · store-detect · client-metrics · portfolio-metrics · ga-overview · clients/metrics); a captured day missing its account row makes those reads skip it and return a STALE (or null) "latest captured date" with NO error. It enumerates distinct captured days per (client,platform) via a LOOSE INDEX SCAN (recursive CTE, one index seek per date = O(distinct-days), NOT O(rows) — a naive GROUP BY/DISTINCT times out on the 4.8M-row heaviest pair; EXPLAIN ANALYZE 122 days in 243ms) and diffs against the account-grain days. RUN 2026-07-23 (read-only): 38,605 (client,platform,date) tuples checked, 733 violations — ALL Shelley Kyle (23c697bb) woocommerce, 2016-10-22..2018-12-10, 1–3 order/product rows/day and no account row; the range ENDS exactly at the 2018-12-10 host-500 deep-backfill wall ([[LORAMER_QUEUE_OF_RECORD]] #7 / the Woo Shelley re-capture stall). GATE-A (real-input, no fixtures, no writes): excluded 3 real account dates from a clean client's comparison set → the detector flagged EXACTLY those 3 and nothing else (PASS). BASELINE AS DATA: scripts/account-row-invariant.baseline.mjs holds the ONE entry as an EXACT (client,platform,[from..to]) window — proven bounded (`--prove-exact` injects a synthetic Shelley woo 2025-06-15 OUTSIDE the range → guard exits 1), never a blanket client+platform mute. SPLIT — CODE-GATE vs DATA-GATE (DELIBERATE, do NOT re-merge): `npm run guard` stays 100% HERMETIC and sits in the Vercel deploy chain (vercel.json has no buildCommand → Vercel runs `npm run build` = `npm run guard && next build`), so a data condition can never brick a deploy. This DB-dependent check runs ONLY via `npm run check:data`, wired into the local pre-push gate (`npm run prepush` = build + check:data) and NEVER into guard/build/Vercel. It FAILS LOUD (exit 2) when SUPABASE_DB_URL is absent — a silent no-op is worse than no check; a missing URL at the pre-push gate means the environment is misconfigured and must stop the push. (An earlier revision wired it INTO guard with a skip-safe fallback for Vercel; that coupled deploys to data state, so it was moved out — the re-merge is what the flag-site + this entry forbid.) ROUTE CALL still standing: a STALE baseline entry (the data got FIXED) is a loud WARNING, not a failure — data improving must never block a push; the entry is then owed removal. The baseline CLEARS when QUEUE #7 re-captures those days. SPAWNED: COVERAGE-FLOOR-CHECK (QUEUE) — coverage.ts minMaxFor() may report Shelley's woo floor as 2018-12-11 (first account row) not 2016-10-22 (first any row), understating her history to Lora. - [DECISION 2026-07-23] **NO GIT PRE-PUSH HOOK — deliberate, not an oversight (pairs with [[LORAMER_ACCOUNT_ROW_INVARIANT_V1]]).** No husky / lefthook / simple-git-hooks and no .git/hooks pre-push was installed to auto-run `npm run check:data` (verified read-only 2026-07-23: no .husky/, .git/hooks holds only inert *.sample templates, core.hooksPath unset, no hook dep). REASON — a push-time trigger is the WRONG trigger for a data-integrity check: data can break with NO commit (a bad backfill, a cron gap, a merchant outage each corrupt metrics_daily without anyone pushing), so a hook firing only on push would miss the exact failures the check exists to catch, and would also bolt a ~13s live-DB scan onto every push. `npm run prepush` (= `npm run build && npm run check:data`) exists as a MANUAL convention; that it is manual is KNOWN, not overlooked. Until the durable answer lands (a SCHEDULE, not a hook — QUEUE ★SCHEDULED-DATA-CHECK), the executor rule in CLAUDE.md (run `npm run check:data` before any push to main + report it) carries it. | 2026-07-23 | do not relitigate.

- [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-23] **LORAMER_LORA_TOOL_DECISION_LOG_V1 — the L2-retrieval instrument stores a FAMILY LABEL, not the question text; migration 044 APPLIED (Gate-A passed).** WHY family, not question_text: chat content already lives LINE-FOR-LINE in client_conversations (role ∈ {user,assistant} + content, one row per turn), so storing the question here would DUPLICATE it — the L2 skip-rate needs only the DECISION + the classified breakdown family. GATE-A (real path, real Opus, writes only the new table): (a) a real turn's logged row MATCHED the model's actual turn-0 decision (family='placement', tool_called + tool_name as observed); (b) with the table renamed away the same turn RETURNED normally — the PGRST205 insert error was console.error'd + swallowed, 0 rows written (proven, not asserted); (c) the rate split captured/narrated correctly over seeds (4/3 skip · 2/1 from-context); (d) the fire-and-forget dispatch adds 0.065 ms/turn on the response path (the insert lands AFTER the response). Migration applied (table empty); the code ships with this commit. | LORAMER_LORA_TOOL_DECISION_LOG_V1, 2026-07-23 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-23] **LORAMER_NEXT_CONV_WRITE_V1 — the -next chat now persists its turns to client_conversations; the redesign was a WRITE ISLAND.** THE ISLAND: dashboard-next mounts ONE chat (ChatLauncher in Shell) with ONE message thread, and it held turns only in React state — ZERO writers. So every -next conversation was invisible to Lora's cross-surface memory recap (/api/intelligence reads client_conversations), invisible to every other surface, and invisible to ★N5 export. Measured cost: the NEWEST client_conversations row before this fix was 2026-06-29 — writing had gone quiet on the legacy surfaces while real use moved to the write-island -next. FIX: both turns POST to the EXISTING /api/conversations (the same path legacy uses — NO new table, NO new endpoint, NO second write path) via one client-side writer src/lib/next/log-conversation-turn.ts. ONE SURFACE VALUE, not three per trigger: NEXT_CHAT_SURFACE='next-ask-lora' covers the pill, the mobile Lora tab, AND the drill-row ✦ because they are one mounted component / one thread — DEVICE IS NOT A SURFACE, and the per-surface Clear (DELETE is keyed by surface) would break if one visible conversation were fragmented across buckets. A drill-opened turn is marked on the EXISTING scope column (scope='drill'), snapshotted at turn start so both turns of the exchange share it — not a second surface. ASSISTANT TURNS ARE LOGGED ONLY ON A GENUINE REPLY (res.ok + d.response): the client-side fallback/error placeholders ("Something went wrong…", "Network error…") are NOT Lora's output and logging them would poison the recap. FAILURE POSTURE mirrors the L2 instrument EXACTLY — fire-and-forget: never awaited, wrapped in try/catch, the fetch rejection swallowed, never throws (legacy AWAITED these writes; this is strictly safer). NO BEHAVIOR CHANGE otherwise (messages array, /api/chat body, system prompt, tools, intelligence path all untouched). GATE-A (real writer fn · real DB · real recap query + real render, sentinels cleaned up): (a) the real writer emits BOTH POSTs with surface='next-ask-lora', correct role/scope/clientId/content, and send() wires user + assistant (assistant gated on res.ok+d.response), un-awaited; (b) with a FAILING fetch (async-reject AND sync-throw) the writer returns void and never throws → the turn's control flow is untouched; (c) a real -next user+assistant pair inserted for The Escential Group appeared in the EXACT intelligence recap query AND rendered into the system-prompt suffix under "PREVIOUS CONVERSATIONS WITH THIS USER" → a -next conversation reaches Lora's cross-surface memory; (d) MOBILE/sm — the writer has zero viewport refs and the mobile Lora tab reaches the same send() with no breakpoint guard, so a turn at sm writes identically (stated limit: no headless browser in the repo, so sm CSS layout was not pixel-asserted). | LORAMER_NEXT_CONV_WRITE_V1, 2026-07-23 | do not relitigate.
- [DIAGNOSIS 2026-07-24 — NOT FIXED, MECHANISM UNIDENTIFIED] **LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_KEYBOARD_BLEED_V1 — the -next Ask-Lora sheet bleeds the page through when the on-screen KEYBOARD is OPEN; NO sixth fix without an identified mechanism.** SYMPTOM (Russ, 2026-07-24, screenshots): with the mobile keyboard raised, page content (ClientPage below) is visible BELOW the chat input bar — the fixed full-screen sheet does not cover the area the keyboard exposes. This reproduces WITH THE KEYBOARD OPEN specifically (distinct from the earlier keyboard-DISMISS pan and from the horizontal wide-answer overflow). CURRENT SHIPPED STATE (verified this session): `.scrim` is `position:fixed; inset:0; z-index:60`; `.panel` is `height:100dvh` at BOTH breakpoints; `visualViewport` is referenced ONLY by the `?debug=chat` readout (LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_DEBUG_V1) — there is NO visualViewport→layout binding. NOT A STACKING-CONTEXT TRAP (verified this session): NONE of the scrim's ancestors (`.subheader` → `.main` → `.body` → `.root` → `<body>`) carries transform / filter / will-change / opacity<1 / backdrop-filter / contain / isolation / perspective (redesign.module.css contains none of them anywhere); the fixed z-index:60 scrim is a ROOT-level layer over static/auto-z page content, so the bleed is NOT a z-index/stacking problem. FIVE PRIOR ATTEMPTS (none fixed the keyboard bleed): (1) LORAMER_MOBILE_BOTTOM_SHEET_V1 (4707757) — legacy /dashboard mobile bottom sheet "preserves visible context"; SHIPPED (legacy surface, not -next). (2) popover bottom-sheet experiment (9af6c92 "never cropped") → REVERTED (1c7273c "breaks desktop"). (3) LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_FLEX_MINWIDTH_V1 (25b4957) — min-width:0 down the flex chain + `.md` table/pre internal scrollers; SHIPPED; fixed the HORIZONTAL wide-answer overflow / send-arrow-off-screen, NOT the keyboard bleed. (4) LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_VISUAL_VIEWPORT_V1 (78de5ab) — bound `.scrim` `--chat-h`/`--chat-top` to `window.visualViewport.height`/`.offsetTop` (100dvh fallback) and `.panel`→`height:100%` → REVERTED (e1bcf75, 2026-07-17), stated reason: "did NOT fix ★UI-OVERFLOW (unchanged on device, Chrome + Safari); an unproven change carried into launch week is pure risk." (5) LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_DEBUG_V1 (130bae0 + 6cace15) — `?debug=chat` in-flow, peak-tracked horizontal-axis readout; SHIPPED; an INSTRUMENT to measure `visualViewport.offsetLeft`, NOT a fix. THE MECHANISM IS NOT YET IDENTIFIED. "dvh does not shrink for the keyboard" is a TRUE banked premise, but the one attempt that acted on it (the visualViewport binding, #4) did NOT fix the symptom on-device — so that premise is NECESSARY-NOT-SUFFICIENT and something else is in play; candidate axes NOT yet proven: iOS position:fixed behavior under the keyboard, the sheet's own scroll/overflow, the `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` input-bar padding, or a mis-identification of what "bleed" actually shows. RULE: NO SIXTH ATTEMPT until the mechanism is identified AND reproduced — five undirected attempts across two sessions is the signature of guessing (cross-ref the RULE-HOME LAW: repeat failure needs an identified cause, not another try). NEXT STEP IS A MEASUREMENT (with the keyboard OPEN: which element is visible, at what coordinates, against visualViewport vs layout viewport) BEFORE any code. This entry RECORDS the diagnosis; it fixes nothing. Cross-ref QUEUE ★UI-OVERFLOW / ★CHAT-GATE-ZINDEX (distinct symptoms). ⚠⚠ **MECHANISM MEASURED AND CONFIRMED 2026-07-26 — THIS ENTRY'S "MECHANISM NOT YET IDENTIFIED" IS SUPERSEDED. The gate it imposed (no sixth attempt without a measurement) is SATISFIED; the measurement is below and the fix is sequenced in two steps.** INSTRUMENT: LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_VIEWPORT_PROBE_V1, server-logged, iPhone iOS 26.2, **CriOS/150 (Chrome on iOS, NOT Safari — the device record said Safari and the UA says otherwise)**, route /dashboard-next/client-profile, 40+ samples. **IT IS BOTH HALVES, AND THEY ARE SEQUENTIAL:** **(a) THE KEYBOARD OCCLUDES, IT DOES NOT RESIZE.** doc.clientHeight held at **766 across every one of 40+ samples** while visualViewport.height fell **766 → 428**. So the LAYOUT viewport never changed and the keyboard occludes **338 layout px**. `.panel` at 100dvh is still 766px tall, so it overhangs the visible window by 338px — the composer and the bottom of the sheet sit UNDER the keyboard. That is the bleed. **(b) iOS AUTO-ZOOMS on the sub-16px input**, ~22ms AFTER the keyboard opens: 19:15:13.685 scale 1 / vv.height 428 / vv.width 440, then 19:15:13.707 **scale 1.1431818008422852** / vv.height 374.39 / vv.width 384.890625 / offsetTop 391.6 / offsetLeft 4.66. **PROOF IT IS A ZOOM AND NOT A RESIZE: 440 ÷ 384.890625 = 1.1431818…, matching the reported scale to SEVEN DECIMALS** — the visual viewport's WIDTH shrank by exactly the scale factor, which only a zoom does. The zoom then compounds (a), shrinking the visible window to ~374 and offsetting it ~350-390px, which is why the scrim reports y = −344 to −392 in visual coordinates. ⛔ **WHY dvh WAS NEVER GOING TO WORK — THIS AVENUE IS CLOSED PERMANENTLY.** dvh tracks the LAYOUT viewport, and the layout viewport never moved (766, constant, all samples). There was nothing for it to track. Do not propose a dvh-based fix for this symptom again. ⛔ **WHY 78de5ab FAILED, SPECIFICALLY.** It bound `--chat-h`/`--chat-top` to visualViewport.**height** and **offsetTop** only, with a 100dvh fallback. It had no knowledge of `scale`, because nothing had ever measured it — so at scale 1.143 the visual viewport is also **12.5% NARROWER (440→385) and horizontally offset (offsetLeft 4.66)**, and a height/top-only binding leaves that axis unhandled. Any step-2 fix that binds height+offsetTop WITHOUT also handling width/offsetLeft is a repeat of 78de5ab and must be refused. ⚠ **THE INSTRUMENT DEFECT THAT NEARLY BANKED A FALSE FALSIFICATION** (fixed same day, LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_PROBE_FREEZE_V1): the on-screen readout LIVE-UPDATED, so the reading relayed from the device — `scale 1.000, vvH 766 = docH = innerH` — was the **focus+600 sample from 19:15:07, SIX SECONDS BEFORE the keyboard opened**. True numbers, wrong phase. It was read as falsifying auto-zoom and an instruction was issued to bank auto-zoom as REJECTED BY MEASUREMENT; the server log said the opposite and the rejection was refused before it was written. THE LESSON, and it is the sharper form of VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT: **an instrument can be correct and still mislead if it does not tell you WHICH PHASE you are looking at.** The readout now LATCHES on the first sample where doc.clientHeight − vv.height > 100 and holds it. ⚠ **UNVERIFIED-BY-US, consistent with our own data:** published reports describe an iOS 26 bug where visualViewport.offsetTop does not reset to 0 after the keyboard is dismissed. Our log shows offsetTop stuck at 344-392 through the entire second episode. **Step 2 MUST NOT assume offsetTop returns to 0 on dismiss.** THE SEQUENCED FIX: STEP 1 = `.input` 14px→16px, removes the ZOOM trigger only (LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_INPUT_16PX_V1, shipped 2026-07-26). STEP 2 = bind the overlay to the visual viewport on height, offsetTop, AND width/offsetLeft with dvh as fallback — authored only after a device reading confirms scale holds at 1.000. **Step 1 alone does NOT close the bleed: at scale 1 with the keyboard up there is still 338px of overhang.** | LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_KEYBOARD_BLEED_V1, 2026-07-24 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION + VERIFIED 2026-07-24] **LORAMER_AGENCY_SCOPE_LORA_V1 — agency / all-clients Lora gets tools + per-call RBAC (was tool-less and printed "Platform: undefined").** THE DEFECT (recon this session): at the all-clients Shell, clientId is empty → the tool gate `clientId && viewerCanAccess(...)` withheld ALL tools (Lora could query nothing), and the fallback prompt rendered `Platform: ${platform}` as the literal "Platform: undefined" (platform is destructured off the request body with no default and -next never sends it). THE RULE (Russ, 2026-07-24, destination — do not re-ask): a question that NAMES a client → Lora answers it using that client's data; a GENERIC question → she asks which client (or says to click into one). CROSS-CLIENT questions ("which client had the best ROAS") are NOT covered by this rule and REMAIN the Agency Lora tier in docs/LORAMER_LORA_SPEC.md — this is the near-term slice, NOT a closed door. THE BUILD (5 parts): (1) route.ts destructures `platform = ''` and the single-client fallback OMITS the Platform clause entirely when absent — never renders "undefined". (2) claude-tools.ts tool gate is now `userEmail ? [tools] : undefined` (the clientId-presence gate is REMOVED) so tools attach at agency scope. (3) RBAC IS EXPLICIT + PER CALL, server-side, in the runClaudeToolLoop EXECUTOR: for every tool_use it resolves the TARGET client = the bound scope client (single-client tab — wins, and the model cannot steer it off) OR, at agency scope, the model-supplied `tu.input.clientId`; then `viewerCanAccess(userEmail, target)` runs and FAILS CLOSED (no target OR no access → an is_error tool_result, the query layer is never reached). This per-call check is now the ONLY thing preventing cross-client access, which is why it lives at the point of use, not at tool-attach time. (4) the agency prompt (buildAgencyScopeContext) carries the RBAC-SCOPED roster (listAccessibleClientsWithNames — reuses listAccessibleClients' owner∪org-grant∪legacy precedence + archived-drop, then names; it can never widen the set) so Lora can resolve a named client — and ONLY clients this viewer can access. (5) each query tool gained an optional `clientId` param (REQUIRED at agency scope, IGNORED at single-client scope). GATE-A (real DB RBAC, real identities, real Opus for the model-behavior cases; query layer a light real client-scoped read; zero writes): (a) agency+OWNER names "The Escential Group" → REAL Opus resolved it to c39ee088 and the executor queried THAT client, answered; (b) agency+generic "how did we do last month?" → REAL Opus called NO tool and asked which client ("Which client would you like me to look at?"); (c) SECURITY, REAL non-owner cote.russell@gmail.com (a real org member granted ONLY Foam OH) forces a query_metrics for Escential c39ee088 → executor's viewerCanAccess DENIES (real resolveAccess → null) → is_error "Access denied", the query layer NEVER touched that client (no leak); (d) the roster for that partial member is EXACTLY [Foam OH], not the owner's other 19 clients; (e) single-client scope unchanged — the bound client is queried and a model-supplied clientId param does NOT steer it off the bound client. tsc 0. WHAT NO STATIC GATE PROVES: that Lora always WORDS the resolve-or-ask correctly — the prompt gives her the rule + the RBAC-scoped roster; the wording is the eval set's question. | LORAMER_AGENCY_SCOPE_LORA_V1, 2026-07-24 | do not relitigate.
- [DECISION 2026-07-24 — LAW, Russ's rule not a proposal] **LORAMER_L4_DETERMINISM_LAW_V1 — L4 determinism = same data + same question → same CONCLUSION, not same wording.** THE DEFINITION: on UNCHANGED data, the same question must reach the same CLAIM. PROSE IS FREE TO VARY; the claim is NOT. The gate tests for CONTRADICTION — opposite DIRECTION, a DIFFERENT entity named as the problem, a threshold crossed differently — NOT identical wording. Two answers can differ and BOTH be right (cut one campaign vs cut two) — that is NOT a failure; only a contradiction is. PREREQUISITE (load-bearing): the JUDGMENT BASIS must be fixed in DATA first — value_model, targets, thresholds. A determinism test over UNSTABLE ground measures the GROUND, not the model — an unset value_model makes "is this ROAS good" non-reproducible BY CONSTRUCTION (see QUEUE ★VALUE-MODEL-COVERAGE-GAP: 11 of 28 active clients have no value_model). ORIGINAL DESIGN RATIONALE, unchanged: THREE NEW TABLES, additive only, no ALTER/DROP/backfill, nothing reads them until the writer ships, so applying the migration alone changes zero behavior; revert = DROP (line items first, FK). `store_orders` + `store_order_line_items` are PLATFORM-AGNOSTIC on the universal key (client_id, platform, account_id) + vendor order id — Shopify and Woo share the SCHEMA and do NOT share an ADAPTER (Shopify via Bulk Operations JSONL, Woo via paged REST + re-fetch-by-id), which is the platform-extensibility law applied as written. `store_bulk_operations` is the third table and is FLAGGED for Russ to strike: the Bulk Operations API is asynchronous and we run serverless, so an op started in one invocation finishes in another, and sync_state has no home for "op gid X is RUNNING". THE LOAD-BEARING DESIGN DECISION: **created_date is adapter-written, NOT a generated column.** Shopify's createdAt carries the SHOP's UTC offset and the live path buckets on the shop-local date; a `generated always as ((created_at at time zone 'UTC')::date)` column would disagree by one day for every late-evening order and every recompute-from-local would then contradict rows forward capture already wrote — byte-identical-to-forward-capture is banked law. created_at_raw preserves the vendor string so a disagreement is provable rather than assumed away. Line items are REPLACE-PER-ORDER inside one transaction, never upserted, because an order edit can REMOVE a line and an upsert only overwrites keys that recur — the same stale-key trap already live at cron/sync:319, deliberately not reproduced at the new grain. Disappearance is detected by last_seen_at + a deleted_upstream_at TOMBSTONE, never by hard-deleting the row: absence must be evidence, not silence. VOLUME MEASURED, not estimated: 20,965 Shopify orders all-time across 7 clients (largest 18,620) + 4,440 Woo = ~25.4k orders fleet-wide, ~46k line items at 1.8/order — roughly 120 MB with raw jsonb, against Supabase Pro's 8 GB included disk, so ~$0 incremental and ~2 orders of magnitude of headroom. Small compute (2 GB RAM, 2-core ARM, $15/mo) is irrelevant to the storage and relevant only to the 8s PostgREST ceiling, which the partial day index exists to keep clear. | LORAMER_ORDER_LEVEL_STORAGE_V1, 2026-07-25 | do not relitigate.

All three overloaded ⇒ 503 + error:'overloaded', never silent degradation. RUSS, VERBATIM: "It depends on the question, AND we have to start moving faster and better because the LIVE environment is in the queue. THREE THINGS THIS SETTLES: (1) NOT EITHER/OR — live-vs-captured is not a pick; where both exist and can differ, BOTH are surfaced, each labeled with its source and its as_of/settlement basis. (2) QUESTION-DETERMINED — which source LEADS is a function of the question asked (a "right now / today" question leads live; a settled-history question leads captured), so the routing is not a fixed global default. (3) BOTH IS THE HONESTY DEFAULT — in most cases both are shown; showing one silently is the failure mode, and it is the same class as the false zero closed by LORAMER_LORA_FETCHERRORS_DEGRADED_V1. ⚠ THIS REFINES A LOCKED DESIGN, stated plainly rather than slipped in: docs/LORAMER_LIVE_BREADTH_UNIFIED_DESIGN.md §4 (LOCKED 2026-06-26) reads "CAPTURED-BY-DEFAULT: Lora answers from the captured system-of-record unless the question is time-sensitive" + offer-live-on-relevance. Russ owns DESTINATION, so this is his call to make and it stands; §4's captured-by-default is hereby the FALLBACK for families that exist in only one source, not the general answer shape. Everything else in that design is UNCHANGED and still governs — Direction B, the provability wall (§2, live writers must never touch the metrics_daily upsert), and above all §3 MERGE WITH MANDATORY PROVENANCE: a BARE combined figure fusing live and captured with no disclosure remains FORBIDDEN, and "BOTH" means both LABELED, never both AVERAGED.
