MEASURED IN PRODUCTION 2026-07-26 on Veterinary mastermind: three chat turns, all HTTP 200, all claude-opus-5, fellBack:false, real output (6,738 / 9,210 / 5,508 tokens). Server durations user-row→spend-row: 105.3s, 125.6s, 78.2s. Only the 78.2s turn persisted (row 529). THREE user rows, ONE assistant row — $1.14 of completed answers destroyed by the client, and Russ re-asked the same question three times. THE FIX: NEW src/lib/chat/persist-assistant-turn.ts — `parsePersistTarget` (the CALLER declares {surface, scope}; no inference from `location`, because legacy surfaces use different surface values and a wrong guess writes into the wrong thread) + `makeAssistantTurnWriter` (a ONE-SHOT latch flipped BEFORE the await, so two concurrent callers cannot both pass). Wired at route.ts on BOTH paths: streaming inside the detached close-path IIFE BEFORE logSpend (the answer must be at least as durable as its cost row), blocking AWAITED before the response returns (logSpend there is fire-and-forget and dies on the serverless freeze). SINGLE WRITER: ChatLauncher's assistant write is DELETED in the same commit; only ChatLauncher sends `persistTurn`, so the two legacy /dashboard callers are byte-identical and keep their own client-side writes on their own surfaces. The USER turn write (ChatLauncher.tsx:182) is UNTOUCHED this slice. GATE-A 29/29 against the REAL module (not a port), including case 3 which REPRODUCES the 07-26 loss pre-patch and shows the same abort landing post-patch — the guard was seen to fail. GATE-B on the REAL route against the REAL prod DB, blocking path: a normal turn wrote exactly one row in the right order; a client aborted at **1,215ms (real AbortError)** still got its answer written server-side 4.2s later (rows 536 user → 537 assistant); no duplicate; a legacy-shaped call with no `persistTurn` wrote NOTHING. ⚠ GATE-B METHOD CAVEAT, stated: run on a live local dev server against the SAME production Supabase (the house pattern — prod's NEXTAUTH_SECRET is unreadable from Claude Code, so a prod-cookie test is impossible); faithful for DB behaviour, does NOT exercise Vercel's function runtime. ⚠ CEILING, stated rather than implied: the latch is PER-INVOCATION. It cannot dedupe across two separate HTTP requests — that needs a turn id and a unique index, i.e. a migration, deliberately out of scope. NOT FIXED and tracked separately: the USER-turn orphan class (★CHAT-USER-TURN-ORPHAN) and the oldest-500 memory freeze (★LORA-MEMORY-OLDEST-500). | LORAMER_CHAT_SERVER_TURN_WRITE_V1, 2026-07-26 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-26 — LAW-ENFORCING] **LORAMER_CHAT_ANSWER_RECOVERY_V1 — the client stops claiming an answer was lost, classifies aborts on the signal it OWNS, and RECOVERS the persisted answer instead of asking for a re-ask.** AMENDS (does not duplicate) LORAMER_CHAT_CLIENT_ABORT_V1 and LORAMER_CHAT_FAILURE_BRANCHES_V1; builds on LORAMER_CHAT_SERVER_TURN_WRITE_V1. THE DEFECT: after slice 1 the answer was durable server-side, but ChatLauncher still rendered "I couldn't reach Lora just now — the connection dropped before I got an answer back" on turns the server COMPLETED and PERSISTED. Observed live twice on 2026-07-26; the copy caused three re-asks at ~$0.50 each. **WEB-FIRST FINDING [VERIFIED 2026-07-26, published sources] — the obvious fix was WRONG.** On iOS Safari an aborted fetch does NOT reliably reject with a DOMException named 'AbortError': WebKit surfaces a generic `TypeError: Load failed`, and it surfaces THE SAME ERROR for genuine network failures, so the two are INDISTINGUISHABLE by the error object. Also string-matching "Load failed" would therefore misclassify real network drops as aborts. Sources: developer.apple.com/forums/thread/771127 · github.com/hotwired/stimulus/issues/782 · trackjs.com/javascript-errors/load-failed/ · developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/AbortSignal. THE CORRECT DISCRIMINATOR is the AbortSignal we own — `controller.signal.aborted`, set synchronously by our own abort() call, spec'd, supported across browsers since 2018. NEVER string-match the message to decide this. THE RECOVERY DISCRIMINATOR, and its stated limit: there is no turn id (that needs a migration), so recovery uses a MONOTONIC WATERMARK — the max conversation row id known before the turn was sent, captured from the hydration read that already runs on open. An assistant row with a HIGHER id was written after we asked, so an older turn can never be re-rendered. ⚠ A WATERMARK IS NOT A TURN ID: if a second tab asked the same client in the same window, its answer would also clear the watermark. That case is DETECTABLE (more than one new assistant row) and is REFUSED with an explicit "I will not guess which one is yours", never guessed. No watermark or no clientId ⇒ no recovery attempted. Recovery is a READ (GET /api/conversations); it NEVER re-POSTs /api/chat, because a silent retry doubles the spend on a turn that most likely already succeeded. GET CONTRACT VERIFIED LIVE before shipping: accepts clientId+surface, returns a NUMERIC monotonic `id`, and is RBAC-scoped by `user_email = session.user.email` (a different caller got 0 rows; no cookie got 401). ⚠ STATED CEILING, not fixed here: that same GET is `.order(created_at asc).limit(200)`, the SIBLING of ★LORA-MEMORY-OLDEST-500 — past 200 rows per (client, user, surface) it returns the OLDEST 200 and recovery would silently find nothing. Measured 2026-07-26: the largest (client, surface) pair is 87 rows, so it does not bite today. TIMERS, revised against MEASURED durations rather than estimates (2026-07-26 production, Veterinary mastermind, user row → awaited spend row): 125.6s · 105.3s · 78.2s, plus a 26.9s Gate-B turn. CHAT_TOTAL_MS 120s→240s (1.91x the worst measured; deliberately UNDER the route's maxDuration of 300s so the SERVER stays the limiter and the client never waits on a killed lambda). CHAT_IDLE_GAP_MS 45s→150s — ⚠ HONEST BASIS: there is NO per-event gap instrumentation, so the only defensible bound is that a silent stretch cannot exceed the whole turn, hence idle ≥ the longest turn measured; 150 is DERIVED from the total-turn bound, NOT measured directly. DUAL-DEADLINE FIX (correct for when streaming returns): the old rearmIdle REPLACED the total cap, so once the first event arrived the absolute ceiling vanished and a stream dripping one byte every 40s ran forever; the deadline is now set once and never re-armed, every re-arm is clamped to it, and the idle timer arms ONLY after the first event so a healthy 200s blocking turn is never killed by an idle gap it was not in. GATE-A 59/59 against the real files; TWO failures found and fixed rather than argued away — idle 120s failed against the 125.6s measurement (raised to 150s), and my first dual-deadline model armed the idle timer from t=0, which would have introduced a regression the old code did not have. GATE-B 17/17 on the real route + real prod DB: a client aborted at 1,211ms with a real AbortError, and recovery FOUND and RENDERED the persisted answer (rows 542 user → 543 assistant), exactly one bubble, checking-copy replaced not appended; an abort with nothing persisted produced honest copy and no phantom; 3 POSTs to /api/chat and 3 spend rows, so recovery cost nothing beyond the original turns. | LORAMER_CHAT_ANSWER_RECOVERY_V1, 2026-07-26 | do not relitigate.

It does NOT fix the iOS keyboard bleed — that mechanism is still unidentified and remains blocked behind LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_KEYBOARD_BLEED_V1's rule (no sixth attempt without a measurement). THE MECHANISMS, identified this session and each fixed at its cause: (1) the chat chain had TWO live vertical scrollers — `.scroll` and the document — and `overscroll-behavior` appeared NOWHERE in the -next chain (repo-wide grep found one hit, in legacy dashboard/page.tsx:109), so a drag past either edge of the message list CHAINED to the page underneath; it also explains the track/thumb mismatch, because the visible scrollbar belonged to the document, whose scroll range is far larger than the list's. FIX: `overscroll-behavior: contain` on `.scroll` PLUS a body scroll lock that restores the PRIOR inline value on close (restoring a hardcoded '' would clobber whatever else owned body.overflow). (2) the chat `.scrim` sat at z-index 60, TIED with `.menu` (redesign.module.css:404), winning only because ChatLauncher renders after TopBar in Shell — an accident of source order, not a design. FIX: 60 → 100, its own band above every fixed layer (.menuBackdrop 55, .drawer 50, nav scrim 40, MobileNav 30). (3) there was NO `viewport` export anywhere in src/app, so Next emitted only its default. FIX: width/initialScale/interactiveWidget:'resizes-content' — ⚠ Chromium/Android ONLY, Safari ignores it; it is NOT an iOS fix. (4) `.head` had no top safe-area inset while the sheet is full-screen, so the header sat under the notch (the composer already cleared the home indicator). (5) the overlay was not portaled — no ancestor defeats position:fixed today (re-verified down the whole chain at HEAD), but a portal makes that permanent instead of a fact that must be re-verified every time Shell changes; `createPortal` preserves React TREE position, so ChatLauncher does NOT remount and the d55f739 cross-client bleed cannot return (asserted in Gate-A). (6) BACK now closes the chat: one tagged history entry pushed on open, popstate closes, and an X/scrim/Esc close CONSUMES our own entry so no phantom survives to swallow a later back press. ⛔ **maximumScale: 1 WAS PROPOSED AND IS REJECTED — DO NOT RE-PROPOSE IT.** It would stop iOS auto-zoom on input focus, but it blocks PINCH-ZOOM for every user, which is a real accessibility regression on a data product built around dense tables. The same result comes from a 16px font-size on the chat input, which is Track 2 and waits on its measurement. Russ ruled on this 2026-07-26. JUDGMENT CALL, OWNED AND STATED: the spec said "exactly ONE scroll container", and the composer `<textarea>` is technically a second one — it grows to max-height then scrolls its own draft. That behaviour is CORRECT (it is P-PL#3's growing input; a textarea that cannot scroll hides text the user typed), so it was KEPT and given the same `overscroll-behavior: contain` rather than removed. The guard counts LAYOUT scrollers and asserts the textarea is contained. GATE-A 32/32 in real headless Chromium at 390x844 and 1280x900 — computed styles, not source text: one layout scroller; overscroll contain on both the list and the textarea; body overflow hidden while open and RESTORED on close; head padding-top resolving through the safe-area calc; scrim z=100 above every other fixed element measured live; nothing position:fixed inside `.panel`; composer static + flex-shrink:0; overlay parented to document.body; the page underneath immobile when the list is pushed past BOTH edges with real forced overflow; back closes without navigating; no phantom entry after an X close; and component state surviving the portal (no remount). LEGACY /dashboard checked because the viewport export is APP-WIDE: same meta, no maximum-scale, no horizontal overflow at 390px, body not locked, route still 200. TWO OF MY OWN GATE-A ASSERTIONS WERE WRONG AND WERE FIXED, NOT ARGUED AWAY: the first required the list to be CURRENTLY overflowing (an empty thread is not, so it counted zero), and the second counted the composer textarea as a layout scroller. GUARD SHIPPED SAME COMMIT (FIX-WITH-GUARD): tests/guards/chat-scroll-chain.guard.mjs, wired into `npm run guard` so Vercel enforces it, PROVEN FAILING against pre-fix HEAD with SIX findings naming each real defect, then passing. It takes LORAMER_GUARD_ROOT so it can be proven against any checkout without touching the working tree. ⚠ GUARD CEILING, stated: static over CSS + component source; it cannot prove what a browser composites and says nothing about the keyboard bleed. Consequence: the probe's own overhang display was computing `docH − vvH` and UNDER-REPORTING. **A number that is a lower bound must never be shown as if it were the value.** **(3) iOS 26 STUCK offsetTop IS CONFIRMED IN OUR OWN DATA**, no longer unverified-by-us: across the whole dismiss burst (20 samples, 21:23:17–21:23:19) `visualViewport.offsetTop` read **374–416 and never returned to 0**. Any future work here must not assume it zeroes out on dismiss. **(4) THE SEQUENCING ARGUMENT HELD, and it is the reusable lesson even though the fix it enabled failed:** killing the zoom FIRST collapsed a two-axis problem to ONE axis (offsetLeft 0, width 440 unchanged). Fixing the smaller measurable thing first is what made the larger problem legible. That remains true; the overlay binding failed for reasons unrelated to it. | LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_KEYBOARD_BLEED_V1, 2026-07-26 | do not relitigate.

- [LESSON-CLASS 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_LAGGING_EVENT_CANNOT_GATE_A_SYNCHRONOUS_CONSUMER_V1 — state derived from an event is stale for everything that runs before the event lands, and the window is big enough to lose in.** The stick-to-bottom pin was updated from the `scroll` event. INSTRUMENTED IN WEBKIT (window.scrollTo wrapped, stacks captured): after a scroll to the top of a 23,650px thread the ResizeObserver callback fired at t+111ms and the scroll event did not arrive until t+134ms — so the follow read a stale `true` in the gap and dragged the user straight back down. The yank arrived THROUGH the machinery built to prevent it. TWO COROLLARIES, both banked: a gate must consult the SYNCHRONOUS truth (here: is the view now above where WE last put it — decidable in the same tick, and immune to "content grew below me", which a distance test is not); and **a pin that governs only the DECISION to scroll, and not the scrolls already scheduled, is not a pin** — `bottom()`'s deferred rAF/+250ms retries had to obey it too. | LORAMER_LAGGING_EVENT_CANNOT_GATE_A_SYNCHRONOUS_CONSUMER_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_SHOPIFY_DEPTH_NOTNULL_FIX_V1 — the Shopify depth write had been 23502-ing every night since 2026-07-19, and the account row's freshness hid it.** ROOT CAUSE, and it is a platform behaviour worth never relearning: **PostgREST builds ONE column list from the UNION of keys across a bulk payload, and any object missing a key that a SIBLING object supplies is sent as an explicit NULL — NOT the column DEFAULT.** `buildShopifyDepthRows` omitted `conversions` on product_type / product_vendor (intelligence-types.ts:418-419 — those two capture shapes carry `{value, netRevenue}` and no unit count, unlike product_tag which has `units`) while every sibling family set it, so Postgres rejected the WHOLE statement: `null value in column "conversions" of relation "metrics_daily" violates not-null constraint`. MEASURED IN PROD off Vercel's 7-day error-cluster table: 32 rejections naming `conversions`, plus 16 naming `spend` on days whose payload also carried a discount/abandoned row that DID set spend — four stores (Foam OH, Influential Drones, The Escential Group, Cozy Foam Factory), thirteen order-days with real money and no depth row beneath them. IT WAS INVISIBLE BY CONSTRUCTION: cron/sync writes the account row and the depth rows as TWO SEPARATE upserts (:319 and :335), the depth one inside its own try/catch that logs and continues — so the account row landed, connection health stayed green, sync_state advanced, cron returned 200, and `cron_runs.error_count` read 23 against 6 errored connections with nobody reading the difference. THE FIX, two files: (1) `conversions: 0` on the two rows — explicit 0, because there is genuinely no count in scope and the KEY'S PRESENCE is the load-bearing part; (2) `normalizeMetricsRows` union-fills any NOT-NULL numeric that a sibling row supplies. **UNION-SCOPED ON PURPOSE — never "always set all six":** filling a column absent from the ENTIRE payload would ADD it to the column list, and on a merge-duplicates upsert that turns a column the writer deliberately left untouched into one overwritten with 0 on every conflict. THE GUARD (same commit, FIX-WITH-GUARD): `tests/guards/metrics-payload-uniformity.guard.mjs`, wired into `npm run guard`. It DERIVES payload shapes BY EXECUTING the five real builders (a text scan cannot see which rows land in the SAME statement), reads the NOT-NULL set from information_schema via a committed snapshot, exempts nullable columns BY NULLABILITY not by name, and covers forward + backfill shapes on all five platforms. PROVEN RED THEN GREEN: pre-fix builder + pre-fix normalize → exit 1, 16 violations; post-fix builder + pre-fix normalize → exit 1, 12 (the guard independently confirming file 1 alone was insufficient); both post-fix → exit 0. EQUIVALENCE PROVEN BEFORE WIDENING THE BLAST RADIUS: the two normalizers are byte-identical on every payload that writes today — 5/8 executed platform payloads identical, the 3 that differ are exactly the 3 that currently throw, and a 60,000-payload property run returned 0 writing-payloads changed. **WOOCOMMERCE CARRIED THE IDENTICAL DEFECT** (spend/impressions/clicks/conversion_value on 3 of 13 rows, and its single-statement upsert would have lost the ACCOUNT row too) — closed by the same normalize change with zero edits to the Woo builder; it had simply never fired because no Woo store has had an order since the writer shipped. | LORAMER_SHOPIFY_DEPTH_NOTNULL_FIX_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.
- [LAW 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_LANDING_IS_THE_ONLY_SHIPPED_V1 — a capture family is not SHIPPED until rows are COUNTED IN metrics_daily. Fetch-measured is not landing-measured; builder-measured is not landing-measured.** PROVENANCE, and it is the whole 2026-07-19 batch: twelve Shopify and ten WooCommerce families were recorded ✅ SHIPPED on evidence that was real, specific, quantitative — and about the wrong thing. Every number banked that day measured the VENDOR RESPONSE or the in-memory aggregation: "measured 7.3× over net", "1,036 points and Shopify REJECTS it", "measured 4.43× over net, up to 11 categories on one product", "0 of 71 tagged", "UNKNOWN came out 0.00%", "reconcile EXACT ($8,609/66 orders)". Not one of them was `select count(*) from metrics_daily where breakdown_type = …`. All twenty-two held ZERO ROWS for eight days and every gate read green, because `check-capture-completeness.mjs` compares a manifest to a registry and never opens the database — which its own header says. THE RULE: the ship report for any capture family states the ROW COUNT, the DATE RANGE and the CLIENT COUNT from metrics_daily, or the family is UNPROVEN and is recorded as such. A probe proves the vendor serves it. A Gate-A proves the builder emits it. Only a row count proves we HAVE it. THE ENFORCER already exists as a queued build: ★DECLARED-VS-LANDED-CHECK (fail when a registry-declared family with a writer has zero rows fleet-wide) — this law is the reason it stops being optional, and per RULE-HOME LAW this entry is the LAST time it may be banked as prose. | LORAMER_LANDING_IS_THE_ONLY_SHIPPED_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

SAME WebKit engine, DIFFERENT BROWSER CHROME, and the difference is exactly where the bugs live: Chrome's bottom toolbar shows and hides on scroll, and it reports visual-viewport transitions differently at the edges — which is the class of every keyboard/composer/clearance bug we have chased. **CONSEQUENCE, stated so it is never quietly forgotten: every prior mobile Gate-B was run in the WRONG BROWSER.** That includes the full-page mobile Lora work and the +308px clearance probe — those results are hereby UNCONFIRMED ON THE REAL TARGET, not wrong, just never tested where it counts. - [PRODUCT POSITION — RUSS, 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_LATENCY_IS_DILIGENCE_V1 — ~10 seconds to an answer is ACCEPTABLE and is positioned as a FEATURE: thorough beats instant. Wrong answers are not acceptable. UNEXPLAINED SILENCE is the actual bug.** THE CONSEQUENCE IS A STANDING PROHIBITION, and it binds route decisions: do NOT cut tool calls, shrink windows, or downgrade the model to shave seconds — that trades away the differentiator to fix a symptom that is not the problem. Lora reads more data than the instant answer can, and that IS the product. What must change is not the duration but the EXPERIENCE of it: a wait with no signal reads as a hang, and a hang reads as broken. The status indicator (★CHAT-STATUS-INDICATOR) is what converts the wait into visible diligence, which is why it sits on the 2026-09-30 demo critical path and why streaming — its prerequisite — stopped being a flag decision. - [PROVEN 2026-07-27 — one live GET, no writes] **LORAMER_META_SILENT_VERSION_UPGRADE_V1 — we send Meta v21.0 and v18.0; Meta serves v25.0 on both, and has been saying so in a header on every single response.** THE PROOF, not an inference: `GET https://graph.facebook.com/v21.0/act_3769140430018695?fields=id,name` (Glass Plus, healthy cohort account) → HTTP 200 with **`facebook-api-version: v25.0`** and **`x-ad-api-version-warning: The call has been auto-upgraded to v25.0 as v21.0 has been deprecated.`** THE SPREAD IS WIDER THAN ONE CONSTANT: 14 files carry v21.0 (meta-ads.ts, meta-intelligence.ts, all 12 backfill writers) and **18 call sites across 9 files still carry v18.0**, including the OAuth callback, the token exchange, the admin token writer and the connection health probe. **META AUTO-UPGRADES PAST-SUNSET CALLS RATHER THAN FAILING THEM, AND THAT IS WORSE THAN FAILING** — the documented "a call to a sunset version fails" behaviour does not hold here, so nothing ever went red, no gate fired, and the URL said one thing while another version answered. Identical shape to LORAMER_SHOPIFY_VERSION_PIN_2026_07_V1 (sent 2025-01, served 2025-10, on all six shops, for months). ⛔ **SEAM CHECK — NO DATA DAMAGE FOUND.** Looked for a definitional step at 2026-06-09 (when everything below v24.0 was deprecated) and at the two pin-edit dates 2026-05-20 and 2026-07-24. **The prime suspect is structurally cleared: Meta is retiring the 7-day-VIEW and 28-day-VIEW windows and WE HAVE NEVER CAPTURED EITHER** — every attribution_window row across all four grains and the family's whole life (2024-11-25→2026-07-26) is one of exactly 28d_click (87,321) · 7d_click (86,980) · 1d_click (86,192) · 1d_view (27,768). A retirement of windows we never held cannot move our numbers. All four survivors continue at flat rows/day across the boundary. The one alarming reading — Veterinary conversions 555→274 on flat spend — is a SPIKE RETURNING TO BASELINE, not a step: deep-before (04-14→05-11) conv/$ 0.1098 · near-before (05-12→06-08) 0.2229 · after (06-09→07-06) **0.1094**, with spend identical to within 0.6% across all three. A definitional seam moves a level and keeps it moved. **LIMITS OF THAT VERDICT, banked so it is never over-read:** only ONE client (Veterinary) has enough conversion volume to detect a level shift at all; the pooled fleet series is CONFOUNDED by cohort growth (5→8→11→13 Meta clients across the window) and was discarded rather than used; `reach` has no testable pre-boundary history (first captured 2026-06-01, eight days before); and Meta's 9-day base restatement re-walk makes the boundary fuzzy by ~9 days. **This can rule out a large, obvious break. It cannot rule out a shift under roughly 20%.** CONSEQUENCE, stated so the work is scoped correctly: **the Meta version work is HYGIENE, not repair.** No data damage found; fix it because an unknown serving version is not an acceptable place to stand, not because the numbers are wrong. | probe 2026-07-27, LORAMER_META_SILENT_VERSION_UPGRADE_V1 | do not relitigate.

The remedy is already proven on one platform: `noteServedApiVersion` in shopifyGraphQL reads the served version on every response, dedupes per (host|sent|served) and console.errors a mismatch, because a mismatch means the pin is a claim rather than a fact. Every platform we integrate gets that instrument or we do not know what we are running. | LORAMER_VERSION_BUMP_IS_A_CAPTURE_EVENT_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — LIVE PATH] **LORAMER_DEGRADED_IS_NOT_FAILED_V1 — a DEGRADED sub-fetch is a partial-capture warning, not a connection failure, and conflating them cost us three days.** THE DEFECT: `finalizeSection` in cron/sync computed `erroredConns = new Set(errsForP.map(e => e.clientId)).size` — every client with ANY entry in `summary.errors` counted as a failed connection. But cron/sync:660 pushes an entry for every DEGRADED sub-fetch, which by design never drops the connection (those family try/catches exist specifically so a partial failure never drops the platform's main rows or its sync_state write). MEASURED: the google 08:08:46Z run reported **17 attempted / 0 succeeded / 17 errored WHILE WRITING 77,647 ROWS**, three days running, because every client logged the same two degradations (audience, conversion_action). FIX: a `connFailed` set keyed `${platform}|${clientId}`, written ONLY by the five outer connection catches; errored counts from that set, degraded is the remainder, and degraded is SURFACED in the JSON summary and a console.warn — never hidden, because it is why conversion_action and audience are thin. Gate-A 30/30 with the classification expression lifted verbatim from the file, proving both directions: the real 08:08 shape now reads 17 succeeded / 17 degraded / 0 errored, AND two real connection failures still read errored 2 / succeeded 15. ⛔ **THE GENERAL RULE, which is the part that outlives this bug: A COUNTER THAT CANNOT DISTINGUISH PARTIAL FROM TOTAL IS WORSE THAN NO COUNTER, because it manufactures alarms that displace real work.** We spent three days reasoning about a fire that was not burning, and the reasoning was careful — the instrument was wrong. Cross-ref LORAMER_INSTRUMENT_MUST_NAME_ITS_PHASE_V1 (an instrument can be correct and still mislead) and the narrow-green enforcers: this is the same family. STILL OPEN: the identical line in cron/catchup:310 (★CATCHUP-COUNTER-CONFLATION), deliberately unfixed. | cron/sync finalizeSection, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.
- [CORRECTED 2026-07-27 — supersedes the 2026-07-24 escalation] **THE GOOGLE QUOTA PICTURE WAS WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION CHANGES WHERE THE WORK IS. FORWARD IS NOT STARVED.** Google forward runs at 08:08 UTC, completes by 08:19, and writes ~77,700 rows/day at **~1,044 GAQL operations across 18 clients — about 7% of the 15,000 Basic cap** — roughly three hours before the quota actually dies (the pause was written 11:31:59Z on 2026-07-27). **THE CONSUMING LANE IS CATCHUP.** It pays the SAME ~58-op per-client breadth fan-out **PER GAP DAY** across a ~35-day window, because it imports the identical DEVICE/GEO/USER_GEO/HOUR/DEMO grain sets and calls the per-Day fetchers once per missing day: **1,187,578 rows on 418 connection-attempts (2026-07-27) against forward's 77,696 on 18.** **THE RESERVE WORKS AS DESIGNED** — `googleForwardReserveDecision` is called from exactly one place, cron/drain:88, and holds the DRAIN off Google for 180 minutes after the reset until forward finishes; it was never built to hold back catchup, and that is not a defect in it. What WAS a defect, fixed this commit: catchup read the global quota pause ONLY in restore mode, so after exhaustion it kept firing every ten minutes until 11:59 (LORAMER_CATCHUP_QUOTA_PAUSE_V1 — the gate at cron/catchup:525 carried the same `restoreMode &&`, so both had to change). ⚠ **CONFIDENCE, stated because it bounds every number above: these are ARITHMETIC over grain-array lengths, not measurements. No per-lane operation counter exists (★GAQL-OP-METER).** THE ONLY FIX THAT REMOVES THE CEILING IS STANDARD ACCESS; everything else is rationing under it. | 2026-07-27 quota recon | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — BACKEND-ISOLATED] **LORAMER_QUOTA_READ_SPLIT_STATE_V1 — the google-quota sentinel read has THREE outcomes, not two, and the two kinds of caller need OPPOSITE defaults when the answer is unknown.** THE DEFECT: `readGoogleQuotaPause` destructured only `data` and threw `error` away. supabase-js NEVER throws — it returns `{ data, error }` — so any read failure produced `data === null`, fell into `if (!data?.backfill_blocked)`, and returned `paused:false`. A FAILED READ WAS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A HEALTHY UNBLOCKED SENTINEL, silently, with no log line on that path. THE FIX: the function returns `state: 'blocked' | 'not_blocked' | 'unknown'` beside `paused`; `paused` keeps its EXACT prior meaning (so the answer path needed no edit), an error returns `paused:false + state:'unknown'` and console.errors it as a DB READ FAILURE explicitly labelled not-a-quota-block, and the clock-based auto-resume branch is unchanged in position and comparison. **CAPTURE LANES HOLD ON UNKNOWN; THE ANSWER PATH STAYS SILENT ON UNKNOWN.** `holdGoogleWork(qp)` = `paused || state==='unknown'` is THE SINGLE PLACE that capture-lane rule lives — cron/drain:77 and cron/catchup:266 gate on it, and no lane may hand-roll the disjunction. /api/intelligence:220 deliberately stays on `.paused`. GUARD: `tests/guards/google-quota-read-fails-open.guard.mjs`, wired into `npm run guard`, hermetic, EXECUTES the real reader AND the real renderers (buildGoogleQuotaLines + buildClaudeContextCacheable); SEEN RED pre-fix (2/6) and mutation-proven in all three directions (fail-open reverted → RED; elapsed-window broken → RED; answer path flipped closed → source pin RED). ⚠ **WHAT IT DOES NOT CLAIM: this makes the fail-open impossible and the failure LOUD; it does NOT prove the fail-open is what fired on 2026-07-28 — that path logged nothing, so the evidence never existed.** See QUEUE ★CATCHUP-PAUSE-NOT-BITING for the observation that settles it. | LORAMER_QUOTA_READ_SPLIT_STATE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [LAW COLLISION 2026-07-28 — RESOLVED, and written down precisely so nobody "simplifies" it back to a boolean] **FAIL-CLOSED EVERYWHERE WOULD HAVE BROKEN A DO-NOT-RELITIGATE LAW, AND THE SPLIT IS WHAT LETS BOTH STAND.** The obvious fix — error → `paused:true` — is correct for the capture lanes and WRONG for the answer path, because `readGoogleQuotaPause` has two kinds of caller: cron/drain and cron/catchup turn the answer into "do I spend Google quota" (a false hold costs one lap, retried in ~10 min, self-healing), while /api/intelligence turns it into a USER-FACING CLAIM — buildGoogleQuotaLines renders "⛔ GOOGLE PLATFORM API QUOTA EXHAUSTED … SAY THIS OUT LOUD" plus an instruction not to offer a retry. Under fail-closed with a null row that block renders with `until` unset, so Lora would tell a user the quota is exhausted until "the next daily reset" when the only thing that happened is a Supabase blip and live Google data is fine. That directly violates **FAIL-PARTIAL READ-PATH LAW** (064dd54, 2026-07-11: read-path endpoints fail PARTIAL + LOUD, never fail-closed-silent) and re-creates the exact incident **VERIFICATION LAW 1** was written from ("Lora: /api/intelligence fail-closed on a network blip"). It also collides with LORA-SEES-EVERYTHING: telling a user their own data is unavailable because OUR read failed is the named failure mode, not a boundary. ALSO CHECKED AND STATED: the `reason` string cannot soften it — both render sites (build-claude-context.ts:538 block, :1340 status ladder) key off `paused` and `until` only and never read `reason`, so a reason naming the read failure would be invisible to Lora and the user alike. THE RULE THAT OUTLIVES THIS: **when one predicate serves both a work-gate and a user-facing claim, it needs two answers, not one default — pick the safe direction PER CALLER, and put the caller rule in one named function so the next lane inherits it instead of re-deciding it.** | LORAMER_QUOTA_READ_SPLIT_STATE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — BACKEND READ-PATH] **LORAMER_SCOPED_DRILLDOWN_FALSE_ZERO_V1 — a scope that cannot reach the data is NOT the data being absent, and the THIRD instance of a class we have now fixed three times.** THE DEFECT, measured: `queryBreakdown` resolved the entity level by probing COARSEST-FIRST and IGNORING `parentEntityId`/`entityId`, then applied the scope filter at that level. Families whose coarsest level happened to match the scope worked (keyword/search_term coarsest=ad_group → 45 and 182 values returned; hour coarsest=campaign → 24). `device` (coarsest=campaign) could NEVER match an ad-group entityId, and campaign-level rows carry `parent_entity_id` = the CUSTOMER id, so a campaign parentEntityId missed too — returning rows=0 plus **"No device data captured for this client in <window>"** while $63,992.80 of device data sat one level away. **THE RULE THIS INHERITS, and the reason it is banked as a CLASS: ABSENCE, FAILURE and ZERO ARE THREE DIFFERENT FACTS, and the caller must be able to tell them apart.** PRIOR INSTANCES, named so the FOURTH is found faster: (1) **LORAMER_WOO_SILENT_ZERO_FIX_V1** (2026-07-25) — a thrown fetch returned a zero-filled SUCCESS object, so forward wrote $0 rows for days the store had sales and eleven breadth families were silently skipped; (2) **query_metrics FALSE-ZERO on pre-data windows** — 0 rows before data start rendered as "$0" instead of "no data". Same disease, three layers. THE FIX, in the preference order Russ set: the level probe now runs WITH the scope predicates applied, so the level is resolved FROM THE SCOPE rather than from coarseness; unscoped calls are byte-identical (the scope-less probe IS the original). When a scope genuinely matches nothing, the query re-probes UNSCOPED and — if the family exists — returns an HONEST SCOPE-LIMITATION note naming the levels where it IS available, plus additive `scopeUnavailable` / `availableEntityLevels` fields, explicitly instructing the reader not to report zero. The old "No <bt> data captured" sentence now fires ONLY when the family truly has no rows at any level. MEASURED AFTER: device unscoped Σ$81,962.98 · scoped to campaign Σ$25,012.53 · scoped to ad group Σ$18,571.83 · scoped to a bogus id → 0 rows + the honest note — i.e. the scope DISCRIMINATES and is not silently ignored. GUARD: `scripts/check-scoped-drilldown.mjs`, 8/8, DB-dependent so deliberately OUT of `npm run guard`/`build` (same posture as check:data). Seen RED pre-fix (2/8, both core) and mutation-proven three ways — probe reverted to ignore scope → RED; honest-note branch forced off → RED; `scopeUnavailable` dropped while keeping the note → RED (a partial fix must not pass). | LORAMER_SCOPED_DRILLDOWN_FALSE_ZERO_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — the non-regression that had to be proven, not assumed] **THE LEVEL PROBE NOW RESOLVES FROM SCOPE, NOT COARSENESS — AND THAT DOES NOT REGRESS LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_LEVEL_SCOPE_V1.** The 2026-07-02 fix exists because `query_breakdown` used to SUM every captured level and DOUBLE-COUNT (google hour $3,945.88 → true $2,427.36; the -next age card ~4x inflated). The obvious way to "make scoped drill-down find the rows" is to widen the level set until something matches — **that would trade a false zero for a false total, which is strictly worse**, because a wrong number is reported with the same confidence as a right one and nothing downstream can tell. THIS CHANGE MOVES *WHICH* SINGLE LEVEL IS CHOSEN; IT NEVER WIDENS THE SET TO MORE THAN ONE. Proven, not asserted: the guard carries a standing assertion that the unscoped google `hour` total lands on the ONE-LEVEL figure (~$81,962.98) and FAILS above $120,000, which is what campaign+ad_group summed would produce. src: 2026-07-28. | do not relitigate.
- [TEST-INFRA CORRECTION 2026-07-28 — recorded because the instrument was wrong and it nearly became a verdict about the model] **THE L10 EVAL QUESTION SCORED LORA AGAINST A NUMBER THE TOOL COULD NOT SERVE.** The L2 harness computed step 5's ground truth ("top device in that ad group" = MOBILE $13,520.48) straight from `metrics_daily` at ad_group level — a row that EXISTS, but that `queryBreakdown` could not return through any argument combination before this fix. So part of that question was unanswerable by construction, and the failing grade was partly an artefact of the harness, not of the answer. **THE RULE: TEST INFRASTRUCTURE CAN BE WRONG, AND A FAILING EVAL QUESTION IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A LORA FAILURE — establish that the answer is REACHABLE THROUGH THE REAL PATH before scoring it.** This is the same family as VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT-BEFORE-TRUSTING-THE-MEASUREMENT, applied to the eval set itself; the same harness had already been caught truncating ground truth at PostgREST's 1000-row ceiling the day it was written. Note what Lora actually did on L10: she opened with "The chain breaks at step 1 — and that invalidates steps 2-6 as asked", named the Google quota outage as the cause, and refused to present figures she could not stand behind. She was handed a false fact by the tool and declined to launder it. src: 2026-07-28 L2 slice 1. | do not relitigate.
- [DECIDED 2026-07-28 — RUSS] **MULTI-ACCOUNT = BOTH. ROLLUP IS THE DEFAULT; PER-ACCOUNT BREAKOUT IS AVAILABLE ON REQUEST.** Closes the open decision at LORAMER_QUEUE_OF_RECORD.md ("MULTI-ACCOUNT rollup-vs-breakout DECISION is OPEN — RUSS'S CALL"), which the 2026-06-05 ROADMAP entry posed and did not resolve. **THE RULE: an all-time or cross-period question SUMS across every account the client holds on that platform and the seam is INVISIBLE — the user must never have to know an account boundary exists in order to get a correct total.** A per-account BREAKOUT is produced when asked for ("which account drove that", "show me each account"), never as the default shape. **DRIVING CASE — Foam OH (957d484e), and the wording matters because a premise was corrected on measurement: the legacy Google account 7688521852 is DORMANT, NOT dead.** It is still CONNECTED (platform_connections row created 2026-05-14, owner cotebrandmarketing@gmail.com, google_tokens row present and refreshing) and the cron is still capturing it daily through 2026-07-27; it simply has no spend since 2026-04-05. **AND THAT IS THE CONCRETE BLOCKER: the dormant account OCCUPIES THE SINGLE `UNIQUE(client_id, platform)` SLOT on platform_connections, so the NEW account cannot be connected at all until that constraint widens** (QUEUE ★MULTIACCOUNT-SLOT-BLOCKER). The clean-break premise otherwise HOLDS on the spend timeline — last spend 2026-04-05, nothing since, no time overlap — so the rollup is a CONCATENATION rather than a merge and the default answer is simply correct. | multi-account decision, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [HAZARD 2026-07-28 — banked with the decision, and it is THREE states, not two] **INSIDE A ROLLUP, ABSENCE AND ZERO MUST STAY DISTINGUISHABLE PER ACCOUNT — AND "ABSENCE" ITSELF SPLITS IN TWO.** The three states a rollup must never collapse: **(a) GENUINELY ZERO** — captured, a real $0 day (Foam OH 2026-06 and 2026-07 are exactly this: 29 and 27 captured days at $0.00); **(b) ABSENT-RECOVERABLE** — not captured, still inside the platform's retention window, so it can be backfilled (Foam OH 2026-05, an entire missing month); **(c) ABSENT-PERMANENT** — not captured and past retention, unrecoverable for all time (Foam OH's ~101 missing days across 2022–2023, all beyond Google's ~37-month floor). Summed blindly, all three read as zero and the rollup reports a confident wrong total. **A PER-ACCOUNT CAPTURE FLOOR IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT: a floor alone can separate "before we started" from "after we started", but it CANNOT tell (b) from (c)** — both are gaps after the floor, and only the platform's retention boundary distinguishes them. Today the floor is scoped per (client, platform), so a rollup starting at the OLD account's floor would silently treat the NEW account's pre-capture days as real zeros. SAME CLASS as the false-zero fixes shipped 2026-07-28 (LORAMER_SCOPED_DRILLDOWN_FALSE_ZERO_V1 — a scope that cannot reach the data is not the data being absent) and 2026-07-25 (LORAMER_WOO_SILENT_ZERO_FIX_V1 — a thrown fetch is not a $0 day), plus the query_metrics pre-data-window fix: ABSENCE, FAILURE and ZERO are different facts, and this decision adds a fourth distinction inside ABSENCE itself. | multi-account hazard, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.

So the only way to reach the heaviest Meta breadth writer was the drain at 4 fires/day × meta cap 4 × a 9-day window ≈ 11 days of history per client per day. **THIS WAS AN OMISSION, NOT A DEFERRAL — no banked decision explains it** (searched DECISIONS + QUEUE; nothing). Meta's own throttle headroom was measured WIDE OPEN at the same time: zero BUC / rate-limit / "reduce the amount of data" signals in the 7-day Vercel error clusters. NEW: `src/app/api/backfill/meta-asset/route.ts`, mirroring /api/backfill/meta-video and /api/backfill/meta-placement exactly (CRON_SECRET bearer auth, clientId/startDate/endDate/dryRun params, stateless bounded-range loop backward from endDate under a 250s budget inside maxDuration 300, `{complete, resumeBefore, subRanges}` contract). ADDED beyond the sibling shape, both deliberate: a FLOOR36 CLAMP computed the same way the drain computes it so the two can never disagree about the wall, and a route-level `plan=true` — necessary because the WRITER'S `dryRun` is NOT a plan (meta-asset-backfill.ts:211 skips only the DB write and still issues every Meta call), so plan mode is the only way to prove boundaries with zero live calls. spend>0 filtering needed nothing: the writer already filters GREATER_THAN 0. GATE-A 5/5, zero Meta calls, zero writes. NOTHING MODIFIED: the drain, drain-registry.ts, meta-asset-backfill.ts, the 9-day drain window and the meta:4 cap are byte-identical; migration 043 remains unapplied. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_DIRECT_ROUTE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [DECIDED 2026-07-28 — the design choice, banked so it is not "simplified" into a shared cursor later] **THE DIRECT ASSET ROUTE IS STATELESS: NO CURSOR, NO sync_state WRITE.** Sharing the drain's `meta_asset` cursor was considered and REJECTED. `rangeLap` (drain-registry.ts:189-205) does an UNGUARDED read-modify-write on `backfill_earliest_date`, and the `__drain_meta` claim leases the CONNECTION for drain laps only — it would not exclude a route. Two writers on one cursor is a lost-update race in BOTH directions: the route could rewind the drain's frontier, or a drain lap could rewind the route's. Going stateless deletes the shared mutable state rather than guarding it, and it is the only race-free option that does not modify the drain. **BOTH SIBLINGS ALREADY DO THIS DELIBERATELY** — meta-video's header says it outright: "NO cursor / NO sync_state — the drain's rangeLap owns the 'meta_video' cursor". THE ACCEPTED COST, stated rather than discovered later: the drain's cursor does not learn what the route captured, so the drain RE-WALKS covered ground. That is WASTEFUL, NOT WRONG — every write is an idempotent upsert on METRICS_DAILY_CONFLICT, so a re-walk rewrites byte-identical rows, and a concurrent drain lap on the same client is therefore safe. Reconciling the cursor afterwards is a separate decision, deliberately NOT made here. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_DIRECT_ROUTE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [PERMANENTLY LOST 2026-07-28 — dated fact, recorded so it is NEVER re-investigated] **FOAM OH META CREATIVE-ASSET GRAIN FOR 2023-06 AND MOST OF 2023-07 IS UNRECOVERABLE.** Client `957d484e-d0c4-4dd0-b382-d8499d556252`, meta account `act_916558565662035`. Meta's ~37-month aggregate wall sat at **2023-07-28** on this date, and the direct route's floor36 clamp refuses to plan a single sub-range older than it (Gate-A: requested 2015-01-01 → effective start 2023-07-28, oldest planned sub-range 2023-07-28). Foam OH's Meta account-grain history begins 2023-06-21, so **2023-06-21 → 2023-07-27 has account totals but will NEVER have creative-asset grain** — roughly **$28.6k of spend** (2023-06 $1,753.83 + 2023-07 $26,870.93) whose creative attribution is gone for good. THE CAUSE IS NOT A DEFECT: the asset families did not exist as capture until M-FILL#1 shipped 2026-07-18, and by then those months were already past or nearly past the wall. ⏳ THE REST OF THE WINDOW IS STILL MOVING: the wall advances ~1 day per day, so 2023-08 ages out ~2026-08, and 2023-11 (a $100,912.73 Black Friday) ages out ~2026-11. Do not re-open this to look for the 2023-06/07 asset rows — they cannot exist. | Foam OH asset-grain loss, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — ADDITIVE BACKEND] **LORAMER_META_ASSET_BUDGET_HEADROOM_V1 — a between-iteration budget check that reserves nothing for the NEXT iteration is not a budget, and it cost two live 504s.** THE DEFECT, in code I wrote the same day: `/api/backfill/meta-asset` checked only `elapsed > BUDGET_MS` between sub-ranges. At t=249s under a 250s budget it PASSED that check, began a fresh 33-report calendar month, and ran past Vercel's `maxDuration = 300` — so the lambda was KILLED and the caller never received `resumeBefore`. **The chaining contract, which is the only thing that makes the route resumable in practice, could not work at all**: every invocation on a heavy client 504s, and no client-side timeout can fix it (the second attempt used --max-time 400 precisely to rule that out, and still took a 504 at 300.34s). **WHY IT DID NOT SURFACE ON THE SIBLINGS I COPIED:** /api/backfill/meta-video runs ~4 reports per lap and meta-placement is similar; assets run 33 (11 breakdowns × 3 entity levels). The same check is safe at 4 and unsafe at 33 — the pattern was inherited without its assumption. THE FIX: measure each lap's duration as it completes, and start another lap only if the remaining budget covers the WORST lap observed so far, or `FIRST_LAP_MS` (90s, deliberately generous) before anything is measured. Extracted as a PURE function `shouldStartAnotherLap` in the new `src/lib/backfill/lap-budget.ts`, so it is provable without a server and without a live Meta call (a Next route file may not export arbitrary symbols). The response now also returns `lapsRun`, `maxLapMs`, `lapMs[]` and `reportsIssued`, because across two live runs the report count was never once observable. ⛔ **THE RULE, which outlives this route: A BETWEEN-ITERATION BUDGET CHECK IS ONLY SAFE IF ONE ITERATION CANNOT EXCEED THE REMAINING CEILING. If an iteration can be long, RESERVE HEADROOM for it — measured from the iterations already run, with a conservative default before anything is measured. Lowering the constant does not fix this; it only changes the size of lap that still overruns.** GATE-A 8/8 pure-function cases incl. the exact observed 504 shape (t=249s, 60s lap → must break); mutation-tested by reverting to the bare `elapsed <= budget` → 4/8 RED, restored byte-exact. NOTHING ELSE TOUCHED: maxDuration unchanged, the drain, drain-registry.ts and meta-asset-backfill.ts byte-identical. ⚠ KNOWN BLIND SPOT, stated not worked around: Meta's `X-Business-Use-Case-Usage` is consumed inside runMetaAssetBackfill and never returned, so this route CANNOT surface throttle status without changing the writer — which was explicitly out of scope. Throttle remains observable only via a writer error or the Vercel error clusters, and after two live runs it is still UNKNOWN. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_BUDGET_HEADROOM_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — SHARED WRITER] **LORAMER_META_ASSET_CODEPOINT_TRUNCATION_V1 — the corruption was OURS, not Meta's: we created an invalid UTF-8 sequence at truncation and Postgres rejected the whole upsert.** THE DEFECT: `meta-asset-backfill.ts:35` capped `breakdown_value` with `String.slice(0, VALUE_CAP)`. JS strings are UTF-16, so `.slice()` cuts by CODE UNIT, not codepoint. Foam OH's body_asset for campaign/2024-11-01 is 307 chars ending "…🟠🟡🟢"; the cut at index 299 landed INSIDE U+1F7E2 (surrogate pair D83D DFE2), keeping the lone high surrogate. JS passes that through `JSON.stringify` unharmed; the UTF-8 encoding on the wire renders it EF BF BD and Postgres rejects the statement with `invalid input syntax for type json` — halting the lap and every family ordered behind body_asset. **VERIFIED THE SOURCE DATA WAS CLEAN: all 53 rows Meta returned for that day were scanned for NUL, lone surrogates and control characters — zero found.** The first hypothesis (a NUL byte in ad copy) was measured and KILLED before any fix was written. ⛔ **THE RULE: ANY CAP APPLIED TO EXTERNAL TEXT MUST CUT BY CODEPOINT.** `[...str].slice(0, cap).join('')` iterates codepoints and can never split a pair; `.slice()` splits silently, with no error and no warning, and the damage surfaces only at a boundary that validates UTF-8. Emoji in ad copy is normal marketing text, not an edge case. FIX: new `src/lib/backfill/safe-truncate.ts` — `capText()` cuts by codepoint, then strips any lone surrogate that was already present, and REPORTS what it changed (`value_truncated` / `value_sanitised` land in `extra`, so sanitisation is never invisible). Wired at meta-asset-backfill.ts:117 and :130. BLAST RADIUS: this writer has two callers — drain-registry.ts:30 (the `meta_asset` step, 4 fires/day across 13 Meta clients) and /api/backfill/meta-asset — so a regression breaks scheduled Meta capture for every client AND the direct route simultaneously. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_CODEPOINT_TRUNCATION_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.
- [GUARD LESSON 2026-07-29 — it cost two mutation rounds to find and it generalises] **A SANITISER DOWNSTREAM OF A DEFECT CAN MASK THAT DEFECT IN TESTS. ASSERT PRESERVATION, NOT JUST VALIDITY.** The first version of `scripts/check-asset-text-cap.mjs` asserted only that capped text contained no lone surrogate and re-encoded as valid UTF-8. It went GREEN under mutation — reverting the codepoint slice to a raw `.slice()` still produced VALID output, because `stripLoneSurrogates` ran afterwards and removed the orphan. The guard could not distinguish "correctly truncated" from "silently dropped the character", **and silent loss is the WORSE failure**: the mutant turns a loud Postgres rejection into quiet data loss that no check would ever surface. FIX: assert the emoji SURVIVES — `value.endsWith('\u{1F7E2}')` and exactly 298 codepoints. Under mutation that now reads `cp=297 endsWith🟢=false valid=true`, i.e. valid AND wrong, which is precisely the state the weak assertion could not see. ⚠ ALSO BANKED, because it nearly shipped a vacuous green: my FIRST mutation attempt used a regex that did not match, so the file was never modified and the guard "passed" against unmutated code. **A MUTATION TEST THAT DOES NOT MUTATE IS WORTH NOTHING — anchor-check that the edit applied before trusting the result.** Same family as VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT-BEFORE-TRUSTING-THE-MEASUREMENT, applied to the guard rather than the data. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_CODEPOINT_TRUNCATION_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.

- ★CHAT-USER-TURN-ORPHAN — **the USER turn is written before the answer exists, so any pre-answer failure leaves a permanent orphan.** ChatLauncher.tsx:182 fires `logNextConversationTurn({role:'user'})` unconditionally BEFORE the fetch. On any turn that dies before an answer exists — 401, the RBAC 404 (chat/route.ts:79), the 503 all-models-overloaded (chat/route.ts:245/352), or a 500 — there will never be an assistant row to pair with it. LORAMER_CHAT_SERVER_TURN_WRITE_V1 does NOT fix this class and was deliberately scoped away from it (Russ's ruling 2026-07-26): the server cannot write an answer that was never produced. CONSEQUENCE: orphaned user turns accumulate in the history and are fed back to Lora as questions she apparently ignored, which is itself a false signal about her behaviour. TWO CANDIDATE FIXES, NEITHER ACTED ON: (a) the server owns BOTH turns — write the user turn in /api/chat after the RBAC gate passes, delete the client write; single writer, correct ordering by construction, but it moves the user turn behind auth so a 401 records nothing at all. (b) the client deletes its own user row on a pre-answer failure — smaller blast radius, but it is a client-side compensating write and dies with exactly the browser this whole class already proved unreliable. - ★WOO-TIER2-BLOCKED-BY-PLATFORM — **STATUS CHANGE 2026-07-25: NOT-STARTED → BLOCKED-BY-PLATFORM.** Woo Tier-2 (change-based restatement on updated_at) is not merely unbuilt — it is **not implementable against the platform as it ships**. VERIFIED 2026-07-25 against the WooCommerce REST API v3 docs: `/orders` has NO native `modified_after` / `modified_before`; that filter comes from a THIRD-PARTY, MERCHANT-INSTALLED WordPress plugin we neither control nor can require, and wc/v3's `after`/`before` filter **date_created**. Our fetcher (woocommerce-intelligence.ts:342-400) is correct as written for that reason, not lagging. The governing law's clause (4) previously asserted the opposite for Woo and is now CORRECTED (DECISIONS LORAMER_RESTATEMENT_WINDOW_LAW_V1 → the [VERIFIED] split; the generalization failure itself is banked as LORAMER_VERIFIED_PLATFORM_SCOPE_V1). CONSEQUENCE, stated not softened: Woo restatement coverage is bounded by whatever created-date window we pick, and **a refund on an order created outside that window is never caught.** THREE NARROWING OPTIONS, none built: (a) match Shopify's 21-day window — one idiom, cheapest, smallest coverage; (b) TWO-TIER — a cheap frequent ~7-day pass plus one daily deeper ~90-day rollback, off-peak and throttled (this is the recorded ROUTE call, DECISIONS 2026-07-25); (c) ★ORDER-LEVEL-STORAGE first, then periodic re-fetch BY ORDER ID, which removes the dependency on a modified-date filter entirely — the only option that restores exactness. UNBLOCKS ONLY VIA (c) or a merchant-side plugin we would have to require, which is not a product we want to sell. src: 2026-07-25 Woo Tier-1 recon. blocked [LC]
- ★ORDER-LEVEL-STORAGE — **NO order-level table exists** (live schema confirmed: no order/line-item/refund/transaction table). Orders are fetched, summed IN MEMORY, and DISCARDED; only daily AGGREGATES persist to metrics_daily. This violates full-grain-with-history: an ORDER is the grain for a store — it is the thing that gets refunded/edited/cancelled — and its absence also BLOCKS change-based restatement, because updated_at tells you an order changed but NOT which day's aggregate to rebuild. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-07-25 — THAT BLOCKING CLAUSE IS FALSE FOR SHOPIFY AND TRUE FOR WOO.** Shopify returns `createdAt` ON THE ORDER NODE (shopify-intelligence.ts:384) and the live capture ALREADY buckets by it (`String(o.createdAt).slice(0,10)`, :938) — a changed order therefore CARRIES its own created day, so a Shopify Tier-2 sweep can name the days to rebuild with no stored orders at all. WOO has no modified-date filter, so there is no sweep to carry a createdAt and the clause holds exactly as written. **SEVENTH INSTANCE TODAY of LORAMER_VERIFIED_PLATFORM_SCOPE_V1 — one platform's constraint written as both platforms'** (same shape as the clause-(4) correction that created that law, and the 'mirrors Woo' comment banked this morning). THE ITEM STILL STANDS ON ITS OTHER LEGS, which is why this is a correction and not a closure: order grain is a standing capture-law violation in its own right, it is the ONLY Woo unblock, and it converts Shopify Tier-2's repair half from a per-day re-fetch into a local recompute. CONSTRAINT: Shopify's query-cost ceiling (OrdersInRange already runs 651/1000 points, LORAMER_SHOPIFY_QUERY_COST_CEILING_V1) bounds how much order/line detail a single query can add. **ESCALATED 2026-07-25 (mechanism decided): the 651/1000 ceiling CANNOT be raised on ANY plan tier (Plus included — confirmed via Shopify's own docs). The correct mechanism is Shopify's BULK OPERATIONS API (no per-query cost cap, built for exactly this kind of large export), NOT a fatter single query. Any future design for this item MUST route through Bulk Operations. Cross-ref ★GRAIN-RETENTION-AUDIT (this is the first confirmed grain-retention miss).** **ALSO THE DURABLE FIX FOR WOO RESTATEMENT (added 2026-07-25):** WooCommerce has no native modified-date filter (★WOO-TIER2-BLOCKED-BY-PLATFORM), so storing the order grain is what makes change detection possible at all — a periodic re-fetch BY ORDER ID needs no `modified_after`, and it restores the exactness the law originally (wrongly) assumed Woo already had. NOT NEW SCOPE: orders are ALREADY fetched, summed in memory, and discarded on both stores, which is a standing capture-law violation this item already exists to close; the Woo restatement dependency is a second reason to do it, not a second item. src: 2026-07-24 restatement recon; 2026-07-25 ceiling confirm + Woo blocker. open [LC]
- ★SHOPIFY-CHANNELINFORMATION-MIGRATION — `Order.channelInformation` (with its `ChannelDefinition`) is DEPRECATED in favor of `Order.attribution`. It still resolves on 2026-07 and returns identical handle::channelName (verified on four live stores 2026-07-26), so this is scheduled, not urgent. IT BACKS A LIVE FAMILY — sales_channel breakdown rows — so it must ship as its OWN change with a before/after proof that the sales_channel values and row counts match, NEVER bundled into a version flip: a wrong channel value and a wrong version would otherwise be indistinguishable. ⚠ COVERAGE GAP TO CLOSE FIRST: no store in the fleet currently has a second sales channel — every order tested was `web::Online Store` or a NULL channel — so the multi-channel path (POS, Shop app, a marketplace) has never been exercised on ANY version. Find or make a multi-channel store before trusting either field. src: 2026-07-26. open [LC]
- ★SHOPIFY-OFFLINE-TOKENS — question banked 2026-07-26, deliberately NOT scoped (DECISIONS entry has the detail). We use ONLINE tokens (~1h) refreshed on demand, so every background capture run performs a token-refresh WRITE and carries refresh-token-rotation risk; two clients (efe036b4, bb9e2c31) are already dead with `refresh_failed - This request requires an active refresh_token` and need a human re-auth. Offline tokens do not expire and would delete this whole failure class. It touches the OAuth install flow on an APPROVED app with a reviewer surface, so it is a DECISION before it is a build. src: 2026-07-26. open [LC]
- ★NEXT-FETCH-CACHE-AUDIT — **flagged 2026-07-26, NOT scoped.** Next.js 14's App Router caches `fetch` GETs by default, and supabase-js reads are GETs — so ANY supabaseAdmin read inside a route handler may be answered from a cached snapshot. Proven live during the order-grain Gate-A: a one-op-per-shop check returned `[]` four times while the identical query returned four rows to curl (DECISIONS LORAMER_ORDER_GRAIN_NOSTORE_READ_V1). Fixed in the order-grain adapter only. THE AUDIT: enumerate every read in a route handler that GATES a subsequent write (claim/lease checks, cursor reads, dedupe checks, presence checks) and decide per site whether it needs `cache: 'no-store'`. Production masks it — fresh process per invocation, and most reads do not gate a write — which is exactly why it would surface as a rare unreproducible wrong answer instead of an error. src: 2026-07-26. open [LC]
- ★ORDER-GRAIN-STEP-2-BACKFILL — next in the ★ORDER-LEVEL-STORAGE sequence. Populate history: Shopify ~21k orders is roughly ONE bulk op per shop; blast radius is still store_* only, but it is the first sustained vendor load. Ships WITH tombstoning/disappearance detection, which was deliberately left out of the writer because it needs a FULL sweep to compare against — a partial-window sweep would tombstone every order outside the window. Gate: Σ stored order subtotal per day ≡ metrics_daily account revenue across real history, the same proof the writer passed on 8 days. src: 2026-07-26. open [LC]
- ★SHOPIFY-TIER2 — change-based (updated_at) restatement, unbounded. NOT STARTED. **BUILDABLE TODAY WITHOUT ORDER GRAIN** (see the ★ORDER-LEVEL-STORAGE correction above), but five gaps were found reading the Tier-1 code on 2026-07-25 and are banked HERE so they are not rediscovered: **(1) STALE KEYS SURVIVE** — cron/sync:319 is a pure `.upsert(onConflict)` with NO delete, so "REPLACE" only replaces keys that RECUR; a day that loses a product/variant/city/channel keeps the old row at its old value forever. Pre-existing in Tier-1; Tier-2 hits it harder because you visit a day precisely BECAUSE something changed. **(2) A DAY THAT DROPS TO ZERO ORDERS IS NEVER CORRECTED** — `bucketByDay` (shopify-intelligence:936-941) is built only from orders the query RETURNED and `onDay` fires per bucket, so zero orders means no callback, no write, and the stale row survives intact. The single most likely Tier-2 trigger — an order cancelled or deleted — is exactly the case this misses. **(3) HARD DELETIONS ARE INVISIBLE** — a deleted order never appears in an updated_at sweep, so nothing signals the day changed at all. Only stored orders make a disappearance detectable (present locally, absent upstream). **(4) FAN-OUT COST** — one window fetch PER distinct affected created day, each 651 of the 1,000-point ceiling; a sweep touching 40 scattered days is 40 fetches, bounded by FORWARD_BUDGET_MS (cron/sync:272) and the throttle. Operational, not correctness. **(5) NO VERIFICATION SURFACE** — without stored orders a re-sum can only be re-fetched and trusted; Tier-1's dry-run drift report (cron/sync:303-311, :363-370) compares AGGREGATE to AGGREGATE and never to orders. NOTE: (1)(2)(3)(5) are all FIXED or made fixable by ★ORDER-LEVEL-STORAGE, which is why the recorded sequence is order grain FIRST. src: 2026-07-25 sequencing recon. open [LC]
- ★SHOPIFY-API-VERSION-SUNSET — **CLOSED 2026-07-26 (DECISIONS LORAMER_SHOPIFY_VERSION_PIN_2026_07_V1).** Pin flipped to 2026-07 on all four sites, permanent served-version instrument + parity guard shipped; the fall-forward was OBSERVED at sent=2025-01/SERVED=2025-10 on all six shops before the flip and sent=2026-07/served=2026-07 after. Original entry follows. **LIVE RISK, found 2026-07-25 during Bulk Operations research; not acted on.** The code pins `GRAPHQL_API_VERSION = '2025-01'` in FOUR places (shopify-intelligence.ts:12, shopify-dimensional-backfill.ts:173, api/shopify/daily:14, api/shopify/callback:9). Per Shopify's versioning doc read today, **2025-01 is no longer a supported version** — supported stable versions are 2025-07 (sunset 2026-07-16, i.e. ALREADY PAST), 2025-10, 2026-01, 2026-04 (current). Shopify does NOT reject a sunset version: it **"falls forward and responds using the oldest accessible stable version."** So every Shopify call we make is being answered by a version we did not choose and have not read the release notes for, while the URL still says 2025-01 — a silent-substitution class we would call a defect anywhere else. Two REST paths still reference 2024-01 in comments only. Not urgent-broken (calls succeed), but it is unlabelled drift on the highest-revenue platform, and any Bulk Operations work must pin a version deliberately: `bulkOperation(id:)` polling and 5-concurrent ops are 2026-01+ features, while `currentBulkOperation` + one-op-at-a-time is the older behavior. Decide the pin BEFORE writing the bulk adapter. src: 2026-07-25. open [LC]

The ATOMIC batch upsert throws "ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE command cannot affect row a second time" and writes NOTHING for the whole batch — every night since 07-14, swallowed by the silent forward-dim catch. Reproduced 2026-07-24 (ga_campaign 07-23, breakdown_value "(not set)" ×2). Bath Fitter's dims are STILL frozen at 07-14; the 7-day lookback (f2a4882) does NOT fix it (the upsert still collides). - ★GOOGLE-QUOTA-PRIORITY-INVERSION — ⛔ **PREMISE FALSIFIED 2026-07-27. REWRITTEN. The old text below is kept as the record of what we believed, not as a description of what is happening.** WHAT IS ACTUALLY TRUE, measured: **forward is NOT starved.** It runs at 08:08 UTC, completes by 08:19, and writes ~77,700 rows/day at ~1,044 GAQL ops across 18 clients — **about 7% of the 15k cap** — three hours BEFORE the quota dies. The "Lora reported Google last captured 07-22" evidence that drove the 2026-07-24 escalation was almost certainly the same counter artifact one layer up: `cron_runs` reported 17 attempted / 0 succeeded / 17 errored on runs that wrote 77,647 rows (DECISIONS LORAMER_DEGRADED_IS_NOT_FAILED_V1, fixed this commit). **THE CONSUMING LANE IS CATCHUP, not the deep geo backfill.** Catchup pays the SAME ~58-op per-client breadth fan-out **PER GAP DAY** across a ~35-day window: 1,187,578 rows on 418 connection-attempts on 2026-07-27, against forward's 77,696 on 18. **THE RESERVE WORKS AS DESIGNED AND IS NOT THE PROBLEM** — `googleForwardReserveDecision` (google-forward-reserve.ts:124) is called from exactly one place, cron/drain:88, and it holds the DRAIN off Google inside a 180-minute window after the reset until forward finishes. It was never built to hold back catchup, and catchup is the lane that burns the cap. PARTIALLY ADDRESSED THIS COMMIT: catchup now honours the global quota pause unconditionally (LORAMER_CATCHUP_QUOTA_PAUSE_V1), which stops the futile spend AFTER exhaustion but does not prevent it. **THE ONLY FIX THAT REMOVES THE CEILING IS GOOGLE STANDARD ACCESS** — unlimited ops at zero Google cost; the blocker is the website-clarification reply Russ still owes. Everything else is rationing. NEXT MEASUREMENT, not a build: ★GAQL-OP-METER, because every op figure above is arithmetic over grain-array lengths and not one of them is measured. — the superseded original follows —

Only Google records sub-fetch degradation; the other four platforms lose it.** LORAMER_LORA_FETCHERRORS_DEGRADED_V1 shipped the RENDERER for `fetchErrors` (Lora now sees per-family "live fetch FAILED, NOT zero, call query_breakdown" instead of an unlabeled empty), and it is keyed on the SHARED PlatformIntelligence field so any platform that starts recording is rendered for free. But only google-intelligence POPULATES it. Meta HAS the signal and THROWS IT AWAY — fetchAllSoft returns `{rows, degraded, detail}` and the caller only console.logs it (meta-intelligence.ts:313-315), so a Meta page-walk cut short by an API error reaches Lora as a short-but-clean list with no degradation marker: the SAME false zero this fix just closed for Google, one platform over. Shopify/Woo/GA have no channel at all — their sub-fetch catches degrade silently. THE WORK: give each fetcher a fetchErrors channel (Meta first — the signal already exists, it just needs to ride the object instead of the log), then the existing renderer + guard cover them with no further prompt work. NOTE the guard already fails the moment a platform starts pushing fetchErrors without a renderer, so this cannot regress in the other direction. src: 2026-07-25 LORA-SEES-EVERYTHING law check. open [LC]

What "yours" legitimately cashes: name/voice/tone · client-specific definitions (net-sales basis, ROAS model) · uploaded documents · retained corrections · guardrails. What it CANNOT cash: a genuinely different model · omniscience · behavioral stability across model upgrades (the Opus 4.8→5 D2 flip is a LIVE example of this exact risk) · true exclusivity (tension with the cross-agency intelligence tier) · ownership/portability. Landing line: "she learns your way of working," not "she's a different AI." Connects to ★LORA-VOICE + ★CHAT-RETENTION-MARKETING — one underlying thread, three separate conversations tonight.
- ★CHAT-RETENTION-MARKETING [LC] — Russ decided 2026-07-24: chat history is DATA, same law as metrics_daily — never deleted, stored FOREVER; the view window is a PRICING TIER, not a delete. State it AFFIRMATIVELY: retention makes Lora smarter for THAT customer (and across an agency's clients) — a benefit, not a buried liability. TWO DISTINCT CLAIMS, different consent requirements — DO NOT BLUR: (a) "your chats make YOUR Lora smarter" — contained per-client/per-agency, safe to state NOW, no extra consent; (b) "your chats make Lora smarter for everyone" — the cross-customer HASHED corpus, requires opt-in consent that does NOT exist yet, do NOT claim until that consent flow ships. Legal wrapper = soft-delete + deleted_at + audit row on a lawful deletion request, same pattern as uploaded docs, gated by ★COHORT-PRIVACY-DOCS.

- SIGN-IN ASKS EVERY USER FOR ADWORDS — auth.ts:23 sends `openid email profile https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords` on EVERY login, so a Shopify/GA-only business owner is asked to approve "See, edit, create, and delete your Google Ads accounts" BEFORE connecting anything. An onboarding/trust problem (worst consent copy in the product, shown to 100% of signups) and it widens the sensitive-scope surface for users who will never touch Google Ads. Fix = decouple adwords from LOGIN (the F3 decoupler route pair already proves the pattern — capture adwords POST-login, on demand); login keeps only openid/email/profile. Own flight, NOT a same-day change: it touches auth.ts (the login path) and the consent screen's scope set → Lesson 42 re-verification. Sequence it AFTER the GA verification clears. src: 2026-07-15 OAuth scope enumeration. open [LC]

- ★ META-BACKFILL-OBSERVED-ZERO — FLEET-WIDE, not one client. FORWARD and BACKFILL disagree about what a dormant day looks like. Forward (meta-metrics-row.ts) pushes the account row UNCONDITIONALLY from totals, so a dormant account still gets a daily $0 anchor row. Backfill (fetchMetaDailyMetrics → runBackfill) writes ONLY the days Meta returns, so a dormant stretch walked by backfill produces NO rows. Same day, two different truths depending on which path saw it. CONSEQUENCES, both observed 2026-07-19: (1) it is why Influential Drones' dormant stretch holds zero rows once forward broke — backfill could never have covered for it; (2) it makes the standard-C landing gate's PARTITIONING anchor untrustworthy, because "an account row exists" stops meaning "we looked at this day" (the false Ennis flag this morning came from an anchor that included days no breakdown could ever exist for). FIX, SCOPED NOT AUTHORED: make Meta backfill emit a $0 account row for days Meta returns empty, matching forward's observed-zero. This is NOT a fabricated zero and does NOT violate the false-zero law — it records the SAME observed-empty that forward already records; the distinction is observed-empty (Meta answered, nothing ran) vs invented (we never asked). BLAST RADIUS: the SHARED backfill path (run-backfill.ts + the meta adapter), so every platform riding that engine must be read first — a read-first scope pass before any authoring, no exceptions. src: 2026-07-19 Meta unseal recon. open [LC]

SUPERSEDES the piecemeal reach/frequency patch (a5c8791). Gate-A: tsc/build/check:data green; v18→v21 value-identical; conversions still restate. done [LC]
- ★ META-API-VERSION-BUMP — the Meta layer runs on Graph API v21.0 (the META_API const in ~10 files) and fetchMetaDailyMetrics was just moved v18.0→v21.0 (LORAMER_META_API_VERSION_BUMP_V1) because v18.0/v19.0 are EXPIRED (Meta silently auto-upgrades expired-version calls to the oldest available version — undocumented + fragile; the v18 account call only "worked" because of this). Current = v25.0 (2026-02); v23.0 already EOL 2026-06-09; v21.0 hits its own 2-year wall ~2026-10. PLAN a deliberate codebase-wide Meta bump v21 → current (v24/v25) as ONE flight, with a per-writer field-shape/value diff (the account fetcher's v18→v21 was value-identical, but a bigger jump must be re-verified) AND a re-confirm that the 2026-06 organic reach-metric retirement stays clear of ad-account reach at the new version. **⛔ UPDATED 2026-07-27 — THE AUTO-UPGRADE IS NO LONGER A THEORY AND THE TIMELINE IS SUPERSEDED. This entry is the ONE HOME for all Meta version work; the two ★ items below are its sub-items, not separate entries (RULE-HOME LAW).** PROVEN by one live GET: we send v21.0, **Meta serves v25.0**, and says so in `facebook-api-version: v25.0` plus `x-ad-api-version-warning: The call has been auto-upgraded to v25.0 as v21.0 has been deprecated.` So "v21.0 hits its own 2-year wall ~2026-10" is WRONG — v21.0 is being auto-upgraded RIGHT NOW, today, and has been since at least the 2026-06-09 deprecation. COUNTS CORRECTED: not "~10 files" — **14 files on v21.0** (meta-ads.ts, meta-intelligence.ts, 12 backfill writers) and **18 call sites across 9 files still on v18.0**. NO DATA DAMAGE FOUND: the seam check found no definitional step at 2026-06-09, 05-20 or 07-24, and the retiring 7d_view/28d_view windows were never captured by us at all — full result and its limits in DECISIONS LORAMER_META_SILENT_VERSION_UPGRADE_V1. **THIS IS HYGIENE, NOT REPAIR** — which is why it ranks POST-9/30 except where noted.
- ★API-VERSION-DRIFT-GUARD — **generalize the guard we already have for one platform to all five.** `tests/guards/shopify-api-version-pin.guard.mjs` exists and guards Shopify's pin; nothing guards the other four, which is how Meta sat on a deprecated version and Google drifted two majors. **WHERE IT LIVES: `check:data`, NOT the build path.** The pins are hermetically greppable from code, but current/sunset needs NETWORK — and a build gate hostage to a vendor's uptime is the wrong gate (same reasoning as ★SCHEMA-SNAPSHOT-DRIFT-CHECK, plus that second failure mode). SOURCES, per vendor: **Meta = the response header itself**, and it is the strongest of the five because it reports what we GOT rather than what is current · **Google = the npm registry JSON for `google-ads-api`** (its major tracks the API major), with sunset derivable as release-month + 12 rather than scraped · **Shopify = `{ publicApiVersions { handle supported displayName } }` on the GraphQL Admin API — the missing release notes do NOT foreclose automating Shopify, because the API answers about itself** · **GA4 = omit entirely**, v1beta is the only version and a permanently-green row is noise · **Woo = do not poll**, it is a read against the merchant's own box and wc/v3 has been the only production namespace for years. TRIGGERS: **WARN** when a pin is behind current. **FAIL at sunset MINUS 30 DAYS, not on the date** — a hard fail on the sunset date is a failure you discover from production breakage, not from the guard. **AND THE META TRIGGER IS DIFFERENT ON PURPOSE: FAIL on `sent ≠ served`, read from the header** — strictly stronger than any sunset check, because Meta auto-upgrades instead of failing, and this trigger would have fired on day one where a date-based check missed it for months. Cross-ref LORAMER_PINS_ARE_WRITTEN_STALE_V1: every stale pin here was written stale, so the guard's real job is to fire at WRITE time, which the sent≠served and behind-current triggers both do. src: 2026-07-27 version-posture recon. open [LC]
- ★ LANDING-GATE-WIRING DEPENDENCY — wire the standard-C landing gate (scripts/check-capture-landing.mjs, untracked at a8db2e6) AFTER M-FILL#8, never before. - GA DISCONNECT LEAVES ga_tokens INTACT — **UNVERIFIED whether capture actually STOPS.** DELETE /api/clients/connections deletes ONE platform_connections row and nothing else: it makes NO call to Google's revoke endpoint (grep for oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke across src/ = zero hits; the only /revoke is the internal /api/org/revoke) and there is NO .delete() on ga_tokens anywhere in src/. The reason is attached here permanently so this is never re-asked without it.** (a) **bb9e2c31 "Escential Group" — CLOSED, no action.** It is the install-fixture DUPLICATE of the real client c39ee088, same shop k9tpib-st.myshopify.com; c39ee088 was reconnected 2026-07-26 and is healthy with data to 2026-07-26. Nothing depends on the duplicate's token. (b) **efe036b4 "LoraMer Demo" — LOAD-BEARING. STAYS ON RUSS'S LIST.** WHY, exactly: its owner `shopify-reviewer@loramer.app` is the Shopify reviewer's ENTRY POINT — the auth.ts `reviewer-token` provider returns that email, and it is hardcoded into `isLegacyCohort` (src/lib/legacy-cohort.ts:9) so it is pinned to the legacy surface by BOTH middleware.ts and preview-gate.ts. DECISIONS "REVIEWER-TOKEN LOGIN…" records the ONGOING Shopify obligation: submitted reviewer test credentials must stay VALID and grant full access to the app's COMPLETE feature set, and that obligation does NOT end at approval. **MEASURED 2026-07-27: that login owns EXACTLY TWO clients — efe036b4 and Test 2 (6e5e441b) — and BOTH hold ZERO metrics_daily rows. A Shopify reviewer signing in today sees an app with no data at all.** The healthy connection to the SAME store hangs off a different client (2fa78486) under a different fixture email the reviewer login cannot see. WHAT IS STILL UNVERIFIED, and only Russ's Shopify Partner dashboard can answer it: whether the obligation binds the LOGIN, the STORE, or both — if it binds only the login, reconnecting efe036b4 may be the wrong repair and re-pointing the reviewer at 2fa78486 may be the right one. Ladder: Free $0/20Q/1-platform/30d/~5k-word-uploads · Starter $49·$470/250Q · Business $99·$950/750Q/+wyws · Pro $299·$2870/2500Q/+priority_support+bulk_export · Agency $199·$1900/500Q/10ws · Scale $999·$9500/2500Q/50ws · Enterprise custom. Breaks no one (0 paid subs; all beta_unlimited). The gate tests for CONTRADICTION (opposite direction / a different entity named as the problem / a threshold crossed differently), NEVER identical text; two answers can differ and both be right (cut 1 vs cut 2 campaigns) — that is not a failure. BLOCKED-BY: the judgment basis must be fixed in DATA first (value_model / targets / thresholds — ★VALUE-MODEL-COVERAGE-GAP); a determinism test over unstable ground measures the ground, not the model.
